\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\left(\tilde{X}_{\mathbf{m},N-n-1}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)\right)

converged to:

(4.94)

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\left(X_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)\right)

Generalizing the notation slightly to clean up the presentation, the
anatomical layout of the limit (
4.93
) is as follows:

(4.95)

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)\tilde{X}_{N-n-1}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

where:

•

(
4.95
) is being considered with respect to a reffinite

R
-frame
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, where
R
is a Dedekind
domain;

•

\left\{M_{n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}
is an
R
-valued
p
-adic M-function;

•

\rho
is a function from
\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
to
R

(the former being identified with both
\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
and
the set
\left\{0,\ldots,p-1\right\}
equipped with the operation
of addition mod
p
);

•

X\in C\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
is
\mathcal{F}
-quasi-integrable,
and:

(4.96)

\tilde{X}_{N-n-1}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)=\sum_{\left|t\right|_{p}\leq p^{N-n-1}}\hat{X}\left(t\right)e^{2\pi i\left\{t\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right\}_{p}}

where
\hat{X}
is
any
Fourier transform of
X
with respect
to
\mathcal{F}
.

Viewed in this way, we want to show is that the limit of (
4.95
)
as
N\rightarrow\infty
is
\mathcal{F}
-convergent to:

(4.97)

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}M_{n}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)X\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

Our next lemma gives us sufficient conditions on
\left\{M_{n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}
,

\mathcal{F}
, and
X
in order to guarantee that we get the convergence
we seek.

Lemma 3

(Main Limit Lemma)
.

Let
R
be a Dedekind domain, let
p

be an integer
\geq 2
, let
\rho:\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow R

be a function, let
\left\{M_{n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}
be a
p
-adic

R
-valued M-function, let
\mathcal{F}
be an evaluative, multiplicative,
reffinite
R
-frame on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, let
X\in C\left(\mathcal{F}\right)

be
\mathcal{F}
-quasi-integrable, and let
\hat{X}
be any Fourier
transform of
X
with respect to
\mathcal{F}
. Fix an integer
n_{0}\geq 1
,
and let
\left\{F_{N}\right\}_{N\geq n_{0}}
be the sequence in

\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R\right)
defined by:

(4.98)

F_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\tilde{X}_{N-n-1}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

Then:

(4.99)

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}F_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}}{=}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)X\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

occurs, and the limit is
\mathcal{F}
-rising-continuous, whenever
the following conditions are satisfied:

I.
\left\{M_{n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}
is strongly
\mathcal{F}
-summable.

II. For each
\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
:

(4.100)

\sup_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\right)<\infty

III. The sequence
\left\{X-\tilde{X}_{N}\right\}_{N\geq 0}
has

\left(\mathcal{F},M\right)
-type decay.

Remark 37
.

Recall that we write:

(4.101)

\Delta_{N}^{\left(m\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}X\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-\tilde{X}_{N}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

for all
m,N\geq 0
and all
\mathfrak{z}
.

Proof: Let everything be as given, and suppose (I), (II), and (III)
all hold. The trick to the proof is to add and subtract
X\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

to the summand of
F_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
:

\displaystyle F_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\overbrace{\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)X\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)}^{\textrm{call this I}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}_{\textrm{call this II}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

Claim 2
.

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\textrm{I}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

is
\mathcal{F}
-convergent. Moreover, letting
\textrm{I}_{\infty}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

denote the function to which the limit
\mathcal{F}
-converges, we
have that:

(4.102)

\textrm{I}_{\infty}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}}{=}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)X\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

and that
\textrm{I}_{\infty}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
is rising-continuous
with respect to
\mathcal{F}
.

Proof of Claim: Fix
\mathfrak{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Since
\rho
’s
domain is finite, for each
\mathfrak{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, there
exists a real constant
\left\|\rho\right\|_{\mathfrak{z}}>0

depending only on
\mathfrak{z}
and
\mathcal{F}
so that:

(4.103)

\sup_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\cdot\right)\right]_{p}\right)\right)\leq\left\|\rho\right\|_{\mathfrak{z}}

Using this in conjunction with (I) and (II), we have that the
n
th
term of
\textrm{I}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
satisfies:

(4.104)

\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)X\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

for some constant depending only on
\rho
and
\mathfrak{z}
. The
strong
\mathcal{F}
-convergence of:

(4.105)

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

given by (I) tells us that the upper bound on the
n
th term of
\textrm{I}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

tends to zero with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}
for each

\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
. When
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}

is non-archimedean, this is sufficient to guarantee the convergence
of
\textrm{I}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
to
\textrm{I}_{\infty}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

in
\mathcal{F}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
as
N\rightarrow\infty
.

On the other hand, if
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}
is archimedean,
(
3
)’s requirement that (
4.105
) converge
absolutely with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}
then shows
that
\textrm{I}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
is dominated by an
absolutely convergent series, and thus that
\textrm{I}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

converges to
\textrm{I}_{\infty}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
in
\mathcal{F}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

as
N\rightarrow\infty
.

Since
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}
is either archimedean or non-archimedean
for each
\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
, and since
\mathfrak{z}

was arbitrary, this proves the
\mathcal{F}
-convergence of
\textrm{I}_{N}

to
\textrm{I}_{\infty}
.

This proves the Claim.
\checkmark

So, all that remains is to show the
\mathcal{F}
-convergence of

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\textrm{II}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

to
0
. To do this, fix
\mathfrak{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, let
N

arbitrary and large, and let
\gamma:\mathbb{N}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{0}

any non-decreasing function satisfying the properties:

(4.106)

\displaystyle\gamma\left(n\right)

\displaystyle\leq n,\textrm{ }\forall n\geq 0

(4.107)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\gamma\left(n\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathbb{R}}{=}+\infty

We then split the
n
-sum in
\textrm{II}_{N}
into two pieces:

\displaystyle\textrm{II}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\overbrace{\sum_{n=0}^{\gamma\left(N\right)-1}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}^{\textrm{Call this }\textrm{III}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{n=\gamma\left(N\right)}^{N-1}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}_{\textrm{Call this }\textrm{IV}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

To see what will be done, let
A_{n}
denote
\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

and let
B_{N,n}
denote
\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
.
As we saw in our investigation of
\textrm{I}_{\infty}
, hypothesis
(I) guarantees that
A_{n}
tends to zero
\mathcal{F}
-wise as

n\rightarrow\infty
.
B_{N,n}
’s behavior, however, is more complicated.
Heuristically, since
n
is relatively small in
\textrm{III}_{N}

(that is,
n<\gamma\left(N\right)
),
A_{n}
will be bounded in

\textrm{III}_{N}
while
B_{N,n}
will be small. On the other hand,
in
\textrm{IV}_{N}
, where
n
is “large” (
n\geq\gamma\left(N\right)
),

A_{n}
will be small and
B_{N,n}
will be bounded. Together, both

\textrm{III}_{N}
and
\textrm{IV}_{N}
end up being small, and
therefore tend to
0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
. The complications
arise from unravelling
B_{N,n}
.

In all of this, note that we can ignore
\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)
,
as it will be bounded by
\left\|\rho\right\|_{\mathfrak{z}}

uniformly with respect to
n
. So, we will.

•
Bounding
\textrm{III}_{N}
: By
hypothesis (I),
\textrm{III}_{N}
’s
A_{n}
is bounded uniformly
with respect to
n
. As for
B_{N,n}
, we have:

(4.108)

B_{N,n}=\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\tilde{X}_{N-n-1}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-X\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

By (III), we know there is a real-valued
p
-adic M-function
\left\{E_{\mathfrak{z},n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}

so that:

(4.109)

\max_{0\leq n\leq\gamma\left(N\right)-1}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\right)\ll\max_{n\leq\gamma\left(N\right)-1}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},N-n-1}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\right)\textrm{ as }N\rightarrow\infty

where the constant of proportionality depends on
\mathcal{F}
and

\mathfrak{z}
, and is
independent
of both
n
and
N
.

Claim 3
.

(4.110)

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\max_{n\leq\gamma\left(N\right)-1}E_{\mathfrak{z},N-n-1}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)\overset{\mathbb{R}}{=}0

Proof of Claim: Since
\left\{E_{\mathfrak{z},m}\right\}_{m\geq 0}

is an M-function,
Proposition
4

allows us to write:

(4.111)

\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},N-n-1}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\right)=\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\frac{E_{\mathfrak{z},N-n-1+n+1}}{E_{\mathfrak{z},n+1}}\right)\overset{!}{=}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\right)}{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},n+1}\right)}

The equality (!) holds because
\mathcal{F}
is multiplicative and
evaluative.

As
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},n}\right)\overset{\mathbb{R}}{=}0
,
since, in
\textrm{III}_{N}
,
n
is restricted to the set
\left\{0,\ldots,\gamma\left(N\right)-1\right\}
,
we have that the value of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},n+1}\right)

will be minimized by setting
n
to be as large as possible; in this
case,
n=\gamma\left(N\right)-1
. Using (
4.111
) on
the right-hand side of (
4.109
) then gives us the upper
bound:

(4.112)

\max_{0\leq n\leq\gamma\left(N\right)-1}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\right)\ll\sup_{n\leq\gamma\left(N\right)-1}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\frac{E_{\mathfrak{z},N}}{E_{\mathfrak{z},n+1}}\right)=\frac{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\right)}{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\right)}

which holds for all sufficiently large
N
. Note the crucial use
of the fact that the constant of proportionality is independent of

n
and
N
.

Finally, to conclude, note that since the
E_{\mathfrak{z},N}
s are
M-functions, there are no
N
and
\mathfrak{y}
for which
E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\left(\mathfrak{y}\right)=0
.
Since
\mathcal{F}
is multiplicative and evaluative, this guarantees
that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\right)\neq 0

for any
N
. Combined with the decay
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\right)\overset{\mathbb{R}}{=}0

required by (III), we see that
\left\{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},n}\right)\right\}_{n\geq 0}

satisfies the hypotheses of
Proposition
30
.

to choose
\gamma\left(N\right)
so that (
4.112
)
tends to
0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
.

This proves the Claim.
\checkmark

Thus, we see that the
n
th term of
\textrm{III}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

is bounded frame-wise by:

(4.113)

\underbrace{\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}_{\textrm{bounded w.r.t. }n}\underbrace{\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}_{\rightarrow 0\textrm{ as }N\rightarrow\infty}

which then decays to
0
. In particular, this shows that:

(4.114)

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\max_{n\leq\gamma\left(N\right)-1}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\mathbb{R}}{=}0

In the case where
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}
is non-archimedean,
this proves the
n
th term of
\textrm{III}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

tends to
0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
uniformly with respect to
n
,
thus making the whole series vanish as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Meanwhile,
in the archimedean case, we can write:

(4.115)

\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\textrm{III}_{N}\right)\ll_{\mathfrak{z}}\underbrace{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{\gamma\left(N\right)-1}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(\cdot\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\cdot\right)\right)}_{\textrm{bounded as }N\rightarrow\infty}\times\underbrace{\max_{0\leq n\leq\gamma\left(N\right)-1}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\right)\left\{X\right\}}_{\textrm{vanishes as }N\rightarrow\infty}

which shows the
\mathcal{F}
-wise vanishing of
\textrm{III}_{N}

as
N\rightarrow\infty
.

•Bounding
\textrm{IV}_{N}
: By
(III), we know there is a real-valued
p
-adic M-function
\left\{E_{\mathfrak{z},n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}

so that:

(4.116)

\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\right)\ll_{\mathfrak{z}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},N-n+1}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\right)

for all
N
and all
n\in\left\{\gamma\left(N\right),\ldots,N-n_{0}\right\}
,
where, as indicated, the constant of proportionality depends only
on
\mathfrak{z}
. Once again, since
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}

is multiplicative and evaluative, we get (
4.111
),
and so, taking the supremum over
n\in\left\{\gamma\left(N\right),\ldots,N-n_{0}\right\}
,
we have:

(4.117)

\sup_{\gamma\left(N\right)\leq n<N}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\right)\ll\sup_{\gamma\left(N\right)\leq n\leq N-1}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\right)}{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},n+1}\right)}\overset{!}{=}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\right)}{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\right)}=1

Here, the equality marked (!) follows from the fact that since (III)
forces
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},n+1}\right)

to tend to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, we can minimize
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(E_{\mathfrak{z},n+1}\right)

by maximizing
N
.

Using (
4.117
), we have:

(4.118)

\max_{\gamma\left(N\right)\leq n\leq N-1}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\ll_{\mathfrak{z}}M_{\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

Thus, if
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}
is non-archimedean, we get
that the
n
th term of
\textrm{IV}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

tends to
0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
, and does so uniformly with
respect to
n
, which then guarantees the convergence of
\textrm{IV}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

to
0
. Alternatively, if
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}
is archimedean,
we can write:

(4.119)

\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\textrm{IV}_{N}\right)\ll_{\mathfrak{z}}\sum_{n=\gamma\left(N\right)}^{N-1}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(M_{n}\right)\leq\sum_{n=\gamma\left(N\right)}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(M_{n}\right)<\infty

for all
N
, using hypothesis (I). As the upper bound vanishes as

N\rightarrow\infty
, this then proves the convergence of
\textrm{IV}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

to
0
in the archimedean case.

Since
\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
was arbitrary,
this shows that
\textrm{II}_{N}
converges to
0
with respect
to
\mathcal{F}
as
N\rightarrow\infty
, and thus
F_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\mathcal{F}
-converges to
F\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
as
N\rightarrow\infty
.

Q.E.D.

4.3.
Assumption
4

& the Inductive Step

With
Lemma
3

now in hand, we know
the conditions required in order to show that the limits of the type
considered in
Assumption
4
hold
rigorously. In order to prove the quasi-integrability of
X_{\mathbf{n}}

and the other
X_{\mathbf{m}}
s, we need to figure out the conditions
needed in order to apply
Lemma
3
,
and then work out the base case of the inductive argument that will
show that the conditions being satisfied for all
X_{\mathbf{k}}

for all
\mathbf{k}<\mathbf{m}
for a given
\mathbf{m}
imply the
necessary conditions are satisfied by
X_{\mathbf{m}}
as well.

So, returning to
Assumption
4
,
there, we claimed that

(4.120)

\tilde{f}_{\mathbf{n},N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\tilde{X}_{\mathbf{m},N-n-1}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

tends to:

(4.121)

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}X_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Here, the convergence will be frame-wise.
Looking at
Lemma
3
’s

hypotheses,
we deduce a collection of conditions sufficient to apply our lemma.

Proposition 31
.

Assumption

4
will hold with
\mathcal{F}
-convergence
whenever there is a multiplicative, evaluative, reffinite
\mathcal{R}
-frame
on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
so that:

I. The M-function
\left(r_{\mathbf{n},0},\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\right)

is strongly
\mathcal{F}
-summable.

II. For each
\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
,
\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\sup_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X_{j}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\right)<\infty
.

III. For each
\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}
,
X_{\mathbf{m}}
is
\mathcal{F}
-quasi-integrable
with a Fourier transform
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}
so that
\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}

has
\left(\mathcal{F},M\right)
-type decay with respect to
N
.

Proof: The only thing that isn’t immediately obvious is (II). Applying

Lemma
3

directly to justify
Assumption

4
would require:

(4.122)

\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\sup_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X_{\mathbf{m}}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\right)<\infty

However, by definition:

(4.123)

X_{\mathbf{m}}=\prod_{j=1}^{d}X_{j}^{m_{j}}

where the
X_{j}
s are degree
1
F-series, each of which is
\mathcal{F}
-compatible.
Since

\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}

is multiplicative, and
since each
X_{j}
is
\mathcal{F}
-compatible, we have:

(4.124)

\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X_{\mathbf{m}}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\right)=\prod_{j=1}^{d}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X_{j}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\right)\right)^{m_{j}}

and so:

\displaystyle\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\sup_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X_{\mathbf{m}}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\right)

\displaystyle=\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\sup_{n\geq 0}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X_{j}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\right)\right)^{m_{j}}

\displaystyle=\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\sup_{n\geq 0}\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X_{j}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\right)\right)^{\Sigma\left(\mathbf{m}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left(\sup_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X_{j}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\right)\right)^{\Sigma\left(\mathbf{m}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\sup_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X_{j}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\right)\right)^{\Sigma\left(\mathbf{n}\right)}

As
\Sigma\left(\mathbf{n}\right)
is finite, we see that
\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\sup_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X_{j}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\right)<\infty

is then sufficient to give us what we need.

Q.E.D.

Of the three conditions given by
Proposition
31
,
(I) and (II) do not require demonstrating, or even
assuming

the quasi-integrability of
X_{\mathbf{m}}
for any
\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}
.
The quasi-integrability of the
X_{\mathbf{m}}
s is only relevant
to (III). As such, our proof of the quasi-integrability of the
X_{\mathbf{m}}
s
will proceed by strong induction with (III). For the inductive step,
we will assume that (III) holds for all proper subsets
\mathbf{m}

of
\mathbf{n}
and then use this to show that (III) holds for
X_{\mathbf{n}}

as well.

To do this, we need to compute
\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}
.
Since
X_{\mathbf{n}}=f_{\mathbf{n}}-g_{\mathbf{n}}
, we tackle
f_{\mathbf{n}}

and
g_{\mathbf{n}}
separately. For
f_{\mathbf{n}}
, we pull out
the
n=0
th through
n=N-2
th terms from its series representation
to write:

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{f_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=f_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)-\tilde{f}_{\mathbf{n},N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\overbrace{\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\left(X_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)\right)}^{\textrm{pull out terms from here to cancel $\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)$ in the line below}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\left(\tilde{X}_{\mathbf{m},N-n-1}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=N-1}^{\infty}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\left(X_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\underbrace{\left(X_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-\tilde{X}_{\mathbf{m},N-n-1}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)\right)}_{\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

We then compute
\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{g_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}

using
Proposition

14
.
Combining this with
\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{f_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}

yields:

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{f_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)-\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{g_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X_{J}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=N-1}^{\infty}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\left(X_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\Delta_{0}^{\left(N\right)}\left\{g_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)+\left[\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1\right]N\beta_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right)r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{N}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)

Now, we take estimates. The various pieces of import are labeled below
using mostly roman numerals

(4.125)

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}\underbrace{\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\overbrace{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)}^{\textrm{ia}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}\overbrace{\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}^{\textrm{ib}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}}_{(\textrm{i}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right))}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\max_{m\geq N}\left(X_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)\right)}_{\textrm{ii}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}\times\underbrace{\sum_{n=N-1}^{\infty}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)}_{\textrm{iii}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left(\Delta_{0}^{\left(N\right)}\left\{g_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)+\left[\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1\right]N\beta_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right)}_{\textrm{iv}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}\times\underbrace{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{N}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)}_{\textrm{v}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

Now, by strong induction,
suppose that the hypotheses of
Proposition

31
hold
. As
\textrm{i}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

is the most complicated of the terms to deal with, we’ll save it for
last. As for the others:

•

\textrm{ii}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
: Because
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)

is a constant and because
\mathbf{n}
has finitely many subsets,
we have:

\displaystyle\overbrace{\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\max_{m\geq N}\left(X_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)\right)}^{\textrm{ii}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\max_{m\geq N}X_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\textrm{ is a seminorm};X_{\mathbf{m}}=\prod_{j=1}^{d}X_{j}^{m_{j}}\right);

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\max_{m\geq N}X_{j}^{m_{j}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\max_{m\geq N}X_{j}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

the bottom line of which is bounded by hypothesis (II) of
Proposition

31
.

•

\textrm{iii}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
: By hypothesis (I) of

Proposition
31
,

\left(r_{\mathbf{n},0},\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\right)
is strongly
\mathcal{F}
-summable,
which gives us the estimate;

(4.126)

\textrm{iii}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\sum_{n=N-1}^{\infty}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{N}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)

the upper bound of which tends to
0
.

•

\textrm{iv}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
: We have:

(4.127)

\textrm{iv}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=g_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)+\left[\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1\right]N\beta_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)

When
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}
is non-archimedean,
\max_{N\geq 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(N\right)<\infty
,
which gives us:

(4.128)

\textrm{iv}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}\max_{n\geq 0}g_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)\textrm{ as }N\rightarrow\infty

Otherwise, when
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}
is archimedean, we have:

(4.129)

\textrm{iv}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}\max\left\{N\left[\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1\right],g_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)\right\}

•

\textrm{v}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
: As
\textrm{v}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{N}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)
,
the assumed validity of hypothesis (I) of
Proposition
31

gives us the desired decay to
0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
.

Putting all of this together, we see that (
4.125
) reduces
to:

(4.130)

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}\underbrace{\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\overbrace{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)}^{\textrm{(ia)}}\overbrace{\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}^{\textrm{(ib)}}}_{\textrm{(i)}}

\displaystyle+\max\left\{N\left[\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1\right],g_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)\right\}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{N}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)

Conveniently, we can deal with both the lower line
and
the
base case of our inductive argument (namely, when
\Sigma\left(\mathbf{n}\right)=1
)
in one fell swoop. This is done by establishing a variant of
Theorem

2
. Here, we will draw from the algebraic
set-up in
Section
3.2
so
as to ensure that all of the arguments to come will work by descent
from an
\mathcal{R}
-frame
\mathcal{F}
to the quotient frame

\mathcal{F}/I
induced by an ideal
I
of a polynomial ring.

Theorem 8
.

Let
K
be a global field, let
p
be an
integer
\geq 2
, let
a_{0},\ldots,a_{p-1}
and
b_{0},\ldots,b_{p-1}

be indeterminates, let
R
denote the polynomial ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}\left[a_{0},\ldots,a_{p-1},b_{0},\ldots,b_{p-1}\right]
,
and let
\mathcal{R}
denote the localization of
R
away from the
prime ideal
\left\langle 1-a_{0}\right\rangle
. Let
\mathcal{F}

be a reffinite
\mathcal{R}
-frame on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, let
\kappa_{X}:\mathbb{N}_{0}\rightarrow\textrm{Frac}R

(where
\textrm{Frac}R
is the field of fractions of
R
) be defined
by:

(4.131)

\kappa_{X}\left(n\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\prod_{j=1}^{p-1}\left(\frac{a_{j}}{a_{0}}\right)^{\#_{p:j}\left(n\right)},\textrm{ }\forall n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

Finally, let
I
be any ideal of
R
so that
\left\langle 1-a_{0}\right\rangle\nsubseteq I

and:

(4.132)

\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\left|a_{0}^{n}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/\mathfrak{I}\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}^{1/n}<1,\textrm{ }\forall\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)

then the following hold:

I. The foliated
p
-adic F-series:

(4.133)

X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\hat{b}_{j}\left(\varepsilon_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)^{j}\right)a_{0}^{n}\prod_{k=1}^{p-1}\left(a_{k}/a_{0}\right)^{\#_{k}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)}

is both compatible and quasi-integrable with respect to
\mathcal{F}/I
,
where:

(4.134)

\hat{b}_{j}=\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}b_{k}e^{2\pi ijk/p}

The formula for
\hat{X}
given in the statement of
Theorem

2
is then a Fourier transform of
X
.
Moreover:

(4.135)

X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=a_{\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]}X\left(\theta_{p}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)+b_{\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]}

holds for all
\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)
. In particular,
we have:

(4.136)

\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}/I}{\ll}a_{0}^{N}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)\textrm{ as }N\rightarrow\infty

II.

(4.137)

\sup_{N\geq 0}\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\right)<1,\textrm{ }\forall\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)

Note that in (
4.132
), the value of the limsup
is dependent on
\mathfrak{z}
, and thus might not be
<1
uniformly
with respect to
\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
. Likewise,
in (
4.137
), the bound is not uniform with
respect to
\mathfrak{z}
, either.

Proof: Let everything be as given.

(
4.132
) implies (
4.135
) by
Theorem

6
, which also gives us the compatibility
of the F-series (
4.133
).

Next, since
\mathcal{F}
is multiplicative, evaluative, and reffinite,
so is
\mathcal{F}/I
, and we have:

(4.138)

\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\right)=\left|X\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)}=\left|X\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

for all
N\geq 0
and all
\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
,
and so, by
Proposition
4
:

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\right)

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left|a_{0}^{n}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left|a_{0}^{n}\frac{\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N+n}}\right)}{\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)}\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

\displaystyle\left(\times\frac{a_{0}^{N}}{a_{0}^{N}}\right);

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left|\frac{a_{0}^{N+n}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N+n}}\right)}{a_{0}^{N}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)}\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

\displaystyle\left(\textrm{re-index}\right);

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{n=N}^{\infty}\left|a_{0}^{n}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}}{\left|a_{0}^{N}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}}

By the hypothesis (
4.132
), the
Root
Test
shows that the series in the numerator converges in
\mathbb{R}
.
Moreover, we get the estimate:

(4.139)

\sum_{n=N}^{\infty}\left|a_{0}^{n}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}\ll_{\mathfrak{z}}\left|a_{0}^{N}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

where, as indicated, the constant of proportionality depends on
\mathfrak{z}
,
though not on
N
. Thus:

(4.140)

\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\right)\ll_{\mathfrak{z}}\frac{\sum_{n=N}^{\infty}\left|a_{0}^{n}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}}{\left|a_{0}^{N}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}}\ll_{\mathfrak{z}}\frac{\left|a_{0}^{N}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}}{\left|a_{0}^{N}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}}=1

Taking the supremum over
N\geq 0
then gives (
4.137
).

Finally, for quasi-integrability, like in
Proposition
13
,
without assuming the
truth
of
Theorem
2
,
we can and very much will
use
the formula it gives for
\hat{X}\left(t\right)
.
Applying
Proposition
13
and
Proposition

14
, we get:

(4.141)

\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\left(\Delta_{0}^{\left(N\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)+\left[\alpha_{X}\left(0\right)=1\right]N\beta_{X}\left(0\right)\right)a_{0}^{N}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)

where:

(4.142)

\Delta_{0}^{\left(N\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=X\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-\tilde{X}_{0}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)=X\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)-\hat{X}\left(0\right)

By (
4.137
), this is bounded frame-wise uniformly
with respect to
N
:

(4.143)

\Delta_{0}^{\left(N\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)+\left[\alpha_{X}\left(0\right)=1\right]N\beta_{X}\left(0\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}/I}{\ll}N\textrm{ as }N\rightarrow\infty

Hence:

(4.144)

\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}/I}{\ll}Na_{0}^{N}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)

By (
4.137
), for each
\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
,

\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(a_{0}^{N}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\cdot\right]_{p^{N}}\right)\right)

tends to
0
at a rate of
O\left(r^{N}\right)
for some
r\in\left(0,1\right)
.
This overwhelms
\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(N\right)

(which is bounded if
\mathfrak{z}
is non-archimedean and is at
most
N
if
\mathfrak{z}
is archimedean), and shows that:

(4.145)

\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}/I}{\ll}Na_{0}^{N}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}/I}{\ll}a_{0}^{N}\kappa_{X}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)

which proves (
4.136
). Taking the limit as
N\rightarrow\infty
,
we get
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}/I}{=}0
,
which proves the quasi-integrability of
X
, as we have just shown
that the Fourier series generated by our choice for
\hat{X}
converges

\mathcal{F}/I
-wise to
X
.

Q.E.D.

Proposition 32
.

Let
R
,
\mathcal{R}
, and an ideal

I\subseteq R
be as given in
Theorem
6
,
and let
\mathcal{F}
be an evaluative, multiplicative, reffinite

\mathcal{R}
-frame on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. If:

(4.146)

\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\left(\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{N}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\cdot\right]_{p^{N}}\right)\right)\right)^{1/N}<1,\textrm{ }\forall\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)

then
g_{\mathbf{n}}
is
\mathcal{F}/I
-quasi-integrable and:

(4.147)

\max\left\{N\left[\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1\right],g_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)\right\}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{N}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)\overset{\mathcal{F}/I}{\ll}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{N}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)\textrm{ as }N\rightarrow\infty

Proof: Apply
Theorem
8
to
g_{\mathbf{n}}
.

Q.E.D.

In our discussion of (
4.130
), the inductive
hypothesis requires us to show that the
\left(\mathcal{F},M\right)
-type
decay of
\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}

for all
\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}
guarantees the same type of decay
will occur for
\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}
.
Since we have already shown that the lower line of (
4.130
)
has
\left(\mathcal{F},M\right)
-type decay, we can get the
\left(\mathcal{F},M\right)
-type
decay of
\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}

by establishing the
\left(\mathcal{F},M\right)
-type decay of
\textrm{i}_{N}

from (
4.130
) and then applying
Proposition

28
to get a single M-function as
an upper bound for the sum of two M-functions.

Thankfully, dealing with
\textrm{i}_{N}
is pretty simple, primarily
because we’ve already done it. Indeed, choosing the term corresponding
to a single
\mathbf{m}
in
\textrm{i}_{N}
, the term we get is
in precisely the same form as the function:

(4.148)

\textrm{II}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)M_{n}\left(\mathcal{\mathfrak{z}}\right)\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

we worked with in our proof of
Lemma
3
.
Recall, there, we showed that:

\displaystyle\textrm{II}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\overbrace{\sum_{n=0}^{\gamma\left(N\right)-1}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)r^{n}\kappa\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}^{\textrm{Call this }\textrm{III}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{n=\gamma\left(N\right)}^{N-1}\rho\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}\right)r^{n}\kappa\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}_{\textrm{Call this }\textrm{IV}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

\displaystyle\ll_{\mathfrak{z}}\frac{E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}{E_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}+r^{\gamma\left(N\right)}\kappa\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{\gamma\left(N\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle=E_{\mathfrak{z},N-\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)+\underbrace{r^{\gamma\left(N\right)}\kappa\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{\gamma\left(N\right)}}\right)}_{\textrm{call this }F_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

\displaystyle=E_{\mathfrak{z},N-\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)+F_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

where
\gamma
was any non-decreasing function
\mathbb{N}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{0}

satisfying:

(4.149)

\displaystyle\gamma\left(n\right)

\displaystyle\leq n,\textrm{ }\forall n\geq 0

(4.150)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\gamma\left(n\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathbb{R}}{=}+\infty

In order to apply
Propositions
26
,
27
,
and
28
, we need for the
multipliers of
E_{\mathfrak{z},N-\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

and
F_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

to satisfy the properties required by those propositions’ hypotheses.
Helpfully, those propositions also show that if said properties are
satisfied by the M-functions that we feed into them, then the M-functions
those propositions give us as our upper bounds will also satisfy those
properties. This is the key to making our inductive step work; not
only does the quasi-integrability of
X_{\mathbf{m}}
for
\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}

imply the quasi-integrability of
X_{\mathbf{n}}
, the decay properties
of the M-function bounds on
\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}

for
\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}
are also passed on to
\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}
.
The trick that makes this all work is restricting our frame’s domain
to
\textrm{NiceDig}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)
, so that we can
apply
Propositions
26
,
27
.

Notation 6
.

Given an
\mathcal{R}
-frame
\mathcal{F}
, let
\left(r,\kappa\right)

be an
\mathcal{R}
-valued
p
-adic M-function with multipliers

r_{0},\ldots,r_{p-1}
, with
r_{0}=r
. Then, for any
\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
,
we write
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(r,\kappa\right)
to denote
the real-valued
p
-adic M-function whose multipliers are
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(r_{0}\right),\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(r_{p-1}\right)
.

Here, then, is the inductive step:

Theorem 9

(The Inductive Step)
.

Let
R
,
\mathcal{R}
, and an ideal

I\subseteq R
be as given in
Theorem
6
,
and let
\mathcal{F}
be an evaluative, multiplicative, reffinite

\mathcal{R}
-frame on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with
D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)\subseteq\textrm{NiceDig}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)
.
Consider the hypotheses:

I.

(4.151)

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\left(\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{N}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\cdot\right]_{p^{N}}\right)\right)\right)^{1/N}<1,\textrm{ }\forall\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)

II.

(4.152)

\max_{j\in\mathbf{n}}\sup_{n\geq 0}\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(X_{j}\circ\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\right)<\infty,\textrm{ }\forall\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)

III. For each
\mathbf{m}
with
\mathbf{0}<\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}
,

X_{\mathbf{m}}
is
\mathcal{F}/I
-quasi-integrable with a Fourier
transform
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}
so that
\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}

has
\left(\mathcal{F}/I,M\right)
-type decay, with:

(4.153)

\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}\overset{\mathcal{F}/I}{\ll_{\mathbf{m}}}E_{\mathbf{m},\mathfrak{z},n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

where, as indicated, the constant of proportionality depends on
\mathbf{m}
,
and where, for each
\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
,

\left\{E_{\mathbf{m},\mathfrak{z},n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}\in\textrm{MFunc}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{R}\right)

so that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}E_{\mathbf{m},\mathfrak{z},n}^{1/n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\mathbb{R}}{<}1

for all non-empty
\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}
.

If (I), (II), and (III) are satisfied, then
X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

is
\mathcal{F}/I
-quasi-integrable with a Fourier transform
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}

so that
\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}

has
\left(\mathcal{F}/I,M\right)
-type decay, with:

(4.154)

\Delta_{N}^{\left(0\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}\overset{\mathcal{F}/I}{\ll}E_{\mathbf{n},\mathfrak{z},n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

where
\left\{E_{\mathbf{n},\mathfrak{z},n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}\in\textrm{MFunc}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{R}\right)

satisfies
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}E_{\mathbf{n},\mathfrak{z},n}^{1/n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\mathbb{R}}{<}1
.

Proof: Let everything be as given and fix
\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
.
Because the arguments apply verbatim when considering descent through
the quotient
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}/I
, we will give
the proof writing
\mathcal{F}
rather than
\mathcal{F}/I
.

By
Proposition
32
, hypothesis (I)
guarantees that the lower line of the key estimate (
4.130
)
tends to
0
frame-wise as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Thus, all that
remains to be done is to show that the top line of that estimate:

(4.155)

\textrm{i}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

tends to
0
with respect to
\mathcal{F}
as
N\rightarrow\infty
.

For brevity, let
\left(\rho_{\mathfrak{z}},\kappa_{\mathfrak{z}}\right)

denote the M-function
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(r_{\mathbf{n},0},\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\right)
.
To complete the proof, we just need to show that
\textrm{i}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

can be bounded frame-wise by a real-valued M-function satisfying the
root test condition (
4.27
).

Proceeding like in
Lemma
3
, we
split
i_{N}
at
n=\gamma\left(N\right)-1
:

\displaystyle\textrm{i}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\gamma\left(N\right)-1}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=\gamma\left(N\right)}^{N-2}r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

Using (
4.153
) and noting the uniform boundedness
of the
r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right]_{p}}
s
with respect to
\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}
and
n
, we have:

(4.156)

\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\max_{n\in S}\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\cdot\right)\right]_{p}}\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}\right)\ll_{\mathfrak{z},\mathbf{n}}\max_{n\in S}\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}E_{\mathbf{m},\mathfrak{z},N-n-1}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

for any set
S
. By
Proposition
29
,
for each
\mathfrak{z}
, we can choose an M-function
E_{\mathfrak{z},n}

so that:

(4.157)

\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}E_{\mathbf{m},\mathfrak{z},n}\left(\mathfrak{y}\right)\leq E_{\mathfrak{z},n}\left(\mathfrak{y}\right),\textrm{ }\forall\mathfrak{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{ }\forall n\geq 0

and so that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}E_{\mathfrak{z},n}^{1/n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)<1
.
Then:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\max_{n\in S}\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(\cdot\right)\right]_{p}}\Delta_{N-n-1}^{\left(n+1\right)}\left\{X_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\ll_{\mathfrak{z},\mathbf{n}}\max_{n\in S}E_{\mathbf{m},\mathfrak{z},N-n-1}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{Proposition}\textrm{ }\mathbf{\ref{prop:Kappa shift equation}}\right);

\displaystyle=\max_{n\in S}\frac{E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}{E_{\mathfrak{z},n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

Thus:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\textrm{i}_{N}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}_{\mathfrak{z},\mathbf{n}}\overbrace{\sum_{n=0}^{\gamma\left(N\right)-1}\rho_{\mathfrak{z}}^{n}\kappa_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\max_{0\leq n\leq\gamma\left(N\right)-1}\frac{E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}{E_{\mathfrak{z},n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}}^{\textrm{Call this }\textrm{III}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{n=\gamma\left(N\right)}^{N-2}\rho_{\mathfrak{z}}^{n}\kappa_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\max_{\gamma\left(N\right)\leq n\leq N-2}\frac{E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}{E_{\mathfrak{z},n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}}_{\textrm{Call this }\textrm{IV}_{N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

Since
\left(r_{\mathbf{n},0},\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\right)
is strongly

\mathcal{F}
-summable, we have that
\rho_{\mathfrak{z}}^{n}\kappa_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)

will be small with respect to
\mathcal{F}
when
n
is large. Note
that as
n\rightarrow\infty
,
1/E_{\mathfrak{z},n+1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

will be maximized when
n
is large, and so, we get the bounds:

(4.158)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\textrm{III}_{N}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}_{\mathfrak{z},\mathbf{n}}E_{\mathfrak{z},N-\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)

(4.159)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\textrm{IV}_{N}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}_{\mathfrak{z},\mathbf{n}}\rho_{\mathfrak{z}}^{\gamma\left(N\right)}\kappa\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{\gamma\left(N\right)}}\right)\frac{E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}{E_{\mathfrak{z},N-1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\ll}_{\mathfrak{z},\mathbf{n}}\rho_{\mathfrak{z}}^{\gamma\left(N\right)}\kappa\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{\gamma\left(N\right)}}\right)

where we have used the fact that
\frac{E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}{E_{\mathfrak{z},N-1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

is going to be equal to one of the multipliers of
\left\{E_{\mathfrak{z},n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}
,
and hence, that it will be bounded independently of
N
and
\mathfrak{z}
.

Writing:

(4.160)

F_{\mathfrak{z},n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\rho_{\mathfrak{z}}^{n}\kappa_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)

we have that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}F_{\mathfrak{z},n}^{1/n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)<1
.

\textrm{i}_{N}
then satisfies the estimate:

(4.161)

\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\textrm{i}_{N}\right)\ll_{\mathfrak{z},\mathbf{n}}E_{\mathfrak{z},N-\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)+F_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

Thus, the desired frame-wise decay of
\textrm{i}_{N}
will follow
once we apply
Propositions
26
,

27
, and
28

to (
4.161
). For this, we just need to be able to choose
a
\gamma
so that the hypotheses of those propositions are satisfied.
This is where the niceness of
\mathfrak{z}
’s digits plays a key
role. We have three cases.

Case 1
: Suppose
\mathfrak{z}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
Since
E_{\mathfrak{z},N}
was constructed so as to guarantee that

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}E_{\mathfrak{z},N}^{1/N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)<1

we have:

(4.162)

1>\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}E_{\mathfrak{z},N}^{1/N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=e_{\mathfrak{z},0}\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\prod_{\ell=1}^{p-1}\left(\frac{e_{\mathfrak{z},\ell}}{e_{\mathfrak{z},0}}\right)^{\#_{p:\ell}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{N}}\right)/N}=e_{\mathfrak{z},0}

Consequently, for all sufficiently large
N
:

(4.163)

E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=e_{\mathfrak{z},0}^{N}\prod_{\ell=1}^{p-1}\left(\frac{e_{\mathfrak{z},\ell}}{e_{\mathfrak{z},0}}\right)^{\#_{p:\ell}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}

Next, since
\left(r_{\mathbf{n},0},\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\right)
is
strongly
\mathcal{F}
-summable, we have that
\left\{F_{\mathfrak{z},n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}=\left(\rho_{\mathfrak{z}},\kappa_{\mathfrak{z}}\right)=\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(r_{\mathcal{\mathbf{n}},0},\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\right)

satisfies:

(4.164)

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\rho_{\mathfrak{z}}^{n}\kappa_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\overset{\mathbb{R}}{=}0

Letting
\rho_{\mathfrak{z},0},\ldots,\rho_{\mathfrak{z},p-1}
be
the multipliers of
\left(\rho_{\mathfrak{z}},\kappa_{\mathfrak{z}}\right)
,
the same argument given for
E_{\mathfrak{z},n}
above shows that

\rho_{\mathfrak{z},k}<1
.

Thus, for all sufficiently large
N
, the upper bound (
4.161
)
then becomes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\textrm{i}_{N}\right)

\displaystyle\ll_{\mathfrak{z},\mathbf{n}}E_{\mathfrak{z},N-\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)+F_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}{E_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}+F_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle\ll e_{\mathfrak{z},k}^{N-\gamma\left(N\right)}+\rho_{\mathfrak{z},k}^{\gamma\left(N\right)}

So, choosing
\gamma\left(N\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\left\lfloor N/2\right\rfloor
,
we have that:

(4.165)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\textrm{i}_{N}\right)

\displaystyle\ll_{\mathfrak{z},\mathbf{n}}\left(\max\left\{\sqrt{e_{\mathfrak{z},k}},\sqrt{\rho_{\mathfrak{z},k}}\right\}\right)^{N}

which shows that at such a
\mathfrak{z}
,
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\textrm{i}_{N}\right)

is bounded by the M-function with
\max\left\{\sqrt{e_{\mathfrak{z},k}},\sqrt{\rho_{\mathfrak{z},k}}\right\}

as its zero multiplier and with all other multipliers being
1
.

Case 2
:

Suppose
\mathfrak{z}\in\textrm{NiceDig}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)\backslash\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
Fix
k\in\left\{0,\ldots,p-1\right\}
, and suppose that
d_{p:k}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=1
.
Then
d_{p:\ell}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=0
for all
\ell\neq k
.
Since
E_{\mathfrak{z},N}
was constructed so as to guarantee that

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}E_{\mathfrak{z},N}^{1/N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)<1

we have:

(4.166)

1>\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}E_{\mathfrak{z},N}^{1/N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=e_{\mathfrak{z},0}\prod_{\ell=1}^{p-1}\left(\frac{e_{\mathfrak{z},\ell}}{e_{\mathfrak{z},0}}\right)^{d_{p:\ell}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}=e_{\mathfrak{z},0}\frac{e_{\mathfrak{z},k}}{e_{\mathfrak{z},0}}=e_{\mathfrak{z},k}

Consequently, there exist real constants
C_{\mathfrak{z}},C_{\mathfrak{z}}^{\prime}>0

so that:

(4.167)

C_{\mathfrak{z}}^{\prime}e_{\mathfrak{z},k}^{N}\leq E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\leq C_{\mathfrak{z}}e_{\mathfrak{z},k}^{N}

for all sufficiently large
N
.

Next, since
\left(r_{\mathbf{n},0},\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\right)
is
strongly
\mathcal{F}
-summable, we have that
\left\{F_{\mathfrak{z},n}\right\}_{n\geq 0}=\left(\rho_{\mathfrak{z}},\kappa_{\mathfrak{z}}\right)=\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(r_{\mathbf{n},0},\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\right)

satisfies:

(4.168)

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\rho_{\mathfrak{z}}^{n}\kappa_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\overset{\mathbb{R}}{=}0

Letting
\rho_{\mathfrak{z},0},\ldots,\rho_{\mathfrak{z},p-1}
be
the multipliers of
\left(\rho_{\mathfrak{z}},\kappa_{\mathfrak{z}}\right)
,
the same argument given for
E_{\mathfrak{z},n}
above shows that

\rho_{\mathfrak{z},k}<1
.

Thus, for all sufficiently large
N
, the upper bound (
4.161
)
then becomes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\textrm{i}_{N}\right)

\displaystyle\ll_{\mathfrak{z},\mathbf{n}}E_{\mathfrak{z},N-\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)+F_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{E_{\mathfrak{z},N}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}{E_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}+F_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle\ll e_{\mathfrak{z},k}^{N-\gamma\left(N\right)}+\rho_{\mathfrak{z},k}^{\gamma\left(N\right)}

again, choosing
\gamma\left(N\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\left\lfloor N/2\right\rfloor
,
we have that:

(4.169)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\textrm{i}_{N}\right)

\displaystyle\ll_{\mathfrak{z},\mathbf{n}}\left(\max\left\{\sqrt{e_{\mathfrak{z},k}},\sqrt{\rho_{\mathfrak{z},k}}\right\}\right)^{N}

which shows that at such a
\mathfrak{z}
,
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{z}}\left(\textrm{i}_{N}\right)

is bounded by the M-function with
\max\left\{\sqrt{e_{\mathfrak{z},k}},\sqrt{\rho_{\mathfrak{z},k}}\right\}

as its zero multiplier and with all other multipliers being
1
.

Case 3
: Suppose
\mathfrak{z}\in\textrm{NiceDig}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)\backslash\mathbb{N}_{0}

and that there is no
k\in\left\{0,\ldots,p-1\right\}
so that
all but finitely many of
\mathfrak{z}
’s digits are equal to
k
.
Then, since
\mathfrak{z}
has nice digits,
d_{p:k}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

exists for all
k
and is not equal to
1
for any
k
. Thus, for
our situation, let
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
be slightly larger
than
\max_{0\leq k\leq p-1}d_{p:k}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
, and
set
\gamma\left(N\right)=\left\lfloor\delta N\right\rfloor
. Then,

\gamma\left(N\right)
,
E_{\mathfrak{z},N-\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\theta_{p}^{\circ\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)
,
and
F_{\mathfrak{z},\gamma\left(N\right)}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

satisfy the hypotheses of
Propositions
26
,

27
, and
28
.

Thus, in either case, (
4.161
) is bounded from above by an
M-function satisfying the necessary decay conditions, and we are done.

Q.E.D.

We now have everything we need in order to prove our main result.
This will be done in two steps. First, we will deal with
\mathcal{R}_{1}
;
this entails showing that for a single F-series
X
, the Fourier
transform ends up inducing an isomorphism out of the polynomial algebra

K\left[X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}\right]
generated
over
K
by the measure
X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}

with pointwise multiplication. For this case—which, for
obvious reasons, I call the
Power Case
—we will
use
Theorem

8
to establish
the quasi-integrability of
X
as the base case, and then apply
Theorem

9
for the inductive step. Once that is done,
we will be able to deal with the general case (
\mathcal{R}_{d}
,
for
d\geq 2
) like so: given a product
X_{1}^{e_{1}}\cdots X_{d}^{e_{d}}

of
d
distinct F-series where the
e_{j}
s are positive integer
exponents, we will set
Y_{j}=X_{j}^{e_{j}}
for all
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}

and then apply
Theorem
9
, using the

Power Case
as the base case to establish the quasi-integrability
of each
Y_{j}
.

Theorem 10

(The Power Case)
.

Let
p\geq 2
, let
K
be a global field
(if
\textrm{char}K>0
, then assume
\textrm{char}K
is co-prime
to
p
). For each
k\in\left\{0,\ldots,p-1\right\}
, let
a_{k}

and
b_{k}
be indeterminates, and let
R_{1}\left(K\right)
(a.k.a.,

R_{1}
,
R
) be the ring of polynomials in the
a_{k}
s and
b_{k}
s
with coefficients in
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, so that
R
is then a free

\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra in
2p
indeterminates. Then, let:

(4.170)

\mathcal{R}_{1}\left(K\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\frac{R\left[x\right]}{\left\langle\left(1-a_{0}\right)x-1\right\rangle}

(a.k.a.,
\mathcal{R}_{1}
,
\mathcal{R}
) be the localization of

R
away from the prime ideal
\left\langle 1-a_{0}\right\rangle
.

Now, let
I
be a unique solution ideal of
R
(
\left\langle 1-a_{0}\right\rangle\nsubseteq I
),
and let
\mathcal{F}
be any multiplicative, evaluative, reffinite

\mathcal{R}
-frame on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with
D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)\subseteq\textrm{NiceDig}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)
.
If:

(4.171)

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\left|a_{0}^{n}\kappa_{1}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)\right|_{\mathcal{F}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}^{1/n}<1,\textrm{ }\forall\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)

where:

(4.172)

\kappa_{1}\left(m\right)=\prod_{k=1}^{p-1}\left(\frac{a_{k}}{a_{0}}\right)^{\#_{p:k}\left(m\right)}

then:

I. There exists a unique rising-continuous function
X\in C\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)

so that:

(4.173)

X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}{=}a_{\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p}}X\left(\theta_{p}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)+b_{\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p}},\textrm{ }\forall\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)

where
\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
is the
completion of
\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}
with respect to the absolute
value
\mathcal{F}/I
associates to
\mathfrak{z}
.

II. For any integer
n\geq 0
, the function
X^{n}\in C\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)

with:

(4.174)

\mathfrak{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\mapsto X^{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\in\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

is
\mathcal{F}/I
-quasi-integrable, where
X^{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

is the pointwise product of
n
copies of
X
, with
X^{n}
being

1
identically when
n=0
.

III. For each integer
n\geq 0
, define
\hat{X}^{*n}:\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\rightarrow\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}\right)\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)

as follows. Let
\hat{X}^{*0}\left(t\right)
denote the function

\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(t\right)
, and let
\hat{X}^{*1}\left(t\right)

denote
\hat{X}\left(t\right)
, given by:

(4.175)

\hat{X}\left(t\right)=\begin{cases}0&\textrm{if }t=0\\
\left(\beta_{1}\left(0\right)v_{p}\left(t\right)+\gamma_{1}\left(\frac{t\left|t\right|_{p}}{p}\right)\right)\hat{A}_{1}\left(t\right)&\textrm{if }t\neq 0\end{cases},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

if
\alpha_{1}\left(0\right)=1
occurs in
\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}
,
and given by:

(4.176)

\hat{X}\left(t\right)=\begin{cases}\frac{\beta_{1}\left(0\right)}{1-\alpha_{1}\left(0\right)}&\textrm{if }t=0\\
\left(\frac{\beta_{1}\left(0\right)}{1-\alpha_{1}\left(0\right)}+\gamma_{1}\left(\frac{t\left|t\right|_{p}}{p}\right)\right)\hat{A}_{1}\left(t\right)&\textrm{if }t\neq 0\end{cases},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

when
\alpha_{1}\left(0\right)\neq 1
in
\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}
.

Then, by recursion, for each
n\geq 2
, let
\hat{X}^{*n}\left(t\right)

denote
\hat{f}_{n}\left(t\right)-\hat{g}_{n}\left(t\right)
, where:

(4.177)

\hat{f}_{n}\left(t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\binom{n}{k}\sum_{m=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-2}\hat{X}^{*k}\left(p^{m+1}t\right)\alpha_{k,n}\left(p^{m}t\right)\prod_{h=0}^{m-1}\alpha_{n}\left(p^{h}t\right)

where:

(4.178)

\alpha_{k,n}\left(t\right)=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}a_{j}^{k}b_{j}^{n-k}e^{-2\pi ijt}

and:

(4.179)

\hat{g}_{n}\left(t\right)=\begin{cases}0&\textrm{if }t=0\\
\left(\beta_{n}\left(0\right)v_{p}\left(t\right)+\gamma_{n}\left(\frac{t\left|t\right|_{p}}{p}\right)\right)\hat{A}_{n}\left(t\right)&\textrm{if }t\neq 0\end{cases},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

when
\alpha_{n}\left(0\right)=1
and:

(4.180)

\hat{g}_{n}\left(t\right)=\begin{cases}\frac{\beta_{n}\left(0\right)}{1-\alpha_{n}\left(0\right)}&\textrm{if }t=0\\
\left(\frac{\beta_{n}\left(0\right)}{1-\alpha_{n}\left(0\right)}+\gamma_{n}\left(\frac{t\left|t\right|_{p}}{p}\right)\right)\hat{A}_{n}\left(t\right)&\textrm{if }t\neq 0\end{cases},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

when
\alpha_{n}\left(0\right)\neq 1
, where, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\alpha_{n},\beta_{n},\gamma_{n},\hat{A}_{n}:\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\rightarrow\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}\right)\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)

are given by:

(4.181)

\alpha_{n}\left(t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}a_{j}^{n}e^{-2\pi ijt}

(4.182)

\beta_{n}\left(t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}c_{n,j}e^{-2\pi ijt}

(4.183)

\gamma_{n}\left(t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\frac{\beta_{n}\left(t\right)}{\alpha_{n}\left(t\right)}

(4.184)

\hat{A}_{n}\left(t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\prod_{m=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1}\alpha_{n}\left(p^{m}t\right)

where:

(4.185)

c_{n,j}\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}-\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\binom{n}{k}a_{j}^{k}b_{j}^{n-k}\hat{X}^{*k}\left(0\right),\textrm{ }\forall n\geq 1,\textrm{ }

In all of the above, all sums are defined to be
0
when the lower
limit of summation is greater than the upper limit of summation. Likewise,
all products are defined to be
1
when the lower limit of multiplication
is greater than the upper limit of multiplication.

As defined,
\hat{X}^{*n}
is then a Fourier transform of
X^{n}

with respect to
\mathcal{F}
.

IV. For all
n\geq 0
,
\hat{X}^{*n}
will be the Fourier-Stieltjes
transform of a distribution in
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}\right)^{\prime}
,
which we identify with the symbol
X^{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}
.
For any integers
m,n\geq 0
, we then define a pointwise product:

(4.186)

\left(X^{m}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}\right)\left(X^{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}X^{m+n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}

This then shows that the distribution
X\left(\mathcal{\mathfrak{z}}\right)d\mathfrak{z}

then generates a free algebra
K\left[X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}\right]

over
K
with respect to pointwise multiplication. Moreover, the
Fourier transform induces an isomorphism from this algebra to the

K
-algebra generated by
\hat{X}
under convolution.

WARNING
: In the convolution algebra generated by
\hat{X}
,
given any integer
n\geq 0
, we
define
the convolution of
n

copies of
\hat{X}
to be
\hat{X}^{*n}
. It will generally not
be the case that computing the actual convolution directly using the
standard definition:

(4.187)

\left(\hat{f}*\hat{g}\right)\left(t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\sum_{s\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{f}\left(t-s\right)\hat{g}\left(s\right)

will yield, for instance,
\hat{X}*\hat{X}=\hat{X}^{*2}
, because
the sum defining the convolution will usually be either divergent
or non-convergent.

Proof: As stated earlier, the proof is by using
Theorem
8

as the base case and then applying
Theorem
9

for
\mathcal{R}_{1}
. This then justifies
Assumption
4
,
which shows that the computations from
Theorem
3

hold true. Here, since
\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}=\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we have
that:

(4.188)

\displaystyle r_{m,n,k}

\displaystyle=\binom{n}{m}a_{k}^{m}b_{k}^{n-m}

(4.189)

\displaystyle r_{n,k}

\displaystyle=a_{k}^{n}

from which we obtain the formulae for
\hat{f}_{n}
,
\hat{g}_{n}
,
and the rest.

Q.E.D.

We now have our main result:

Corollary
.

Let
d,p
be positive integers, with
d\geq 1

and
p\geq 2
, let
K
be a global field (if
\textrm{char}K>0
,
then assume
\textrm{char}K
is co-prime to
p
). For each
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}

and
k\in\left\{0,\ldots,p-1\right\}
let
a_{j,k}
and
b_{j,k}

be indeterminates, and let
R_{d}\left(K\right)
(a.k.a.,
R_{d}
,

R
) be the ring of polynomials in the
a_{j,k}
s and
b_{j,k}
s
with coefficients in
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, so that
R
is then a free

\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra in
2dp
indeterminates. Then, let:

(4.190)

\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(K\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\frac{R\left[x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}\right]}{\left\langle\left(1-a_{1,0}\right)x_{1}-1,\ldots,\left(1-a_{d,0}\right)x_{d}-1\right\rangle}

(a.k.a.,
\mathcal{R}_{d}
,
\mathcal{R}
) be the localization of

R
away from the prime ideal
\left\langle 1-a_{1,0},\ldots,1-a_{d,0}\right\rangle
.

Writing:

(4.191)

\kappa_{j}\left(n\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\prod_{k=1}^{p-1}\left(\frac{a_{j,k}}{a_{j,0}}\right)^{\#_{p:k}\left(n\right)}

let
\mathcal{F}
be a digital
\mathcal{R}
-frame on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}

so that:

(4.192)

\left|a_{j,0}\right|_{\mathcal{F}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\prod_{k=1}^{p-1}\left|\frac{a_{j,k}}{a_{j,0}}\right|_{\mathcal{F}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}^{\#_{p:k}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)}<1,\textrm{ }\forall\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}\right),\textrm{ }\forall j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}

Let
I
be a unique solution ideal of
R
(
\left\langle 1-a_{j,0}\right\rangle\cap I=\left\{0\right\}

for all
j
). Then:

I. For each
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
, there
exists a unique rising-continuous function
X_{j}\in C\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)

so that:

(4.193)

X_{j}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}{=}a_{j,\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p}}X_{j}\left(\theta_{p}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)+b_{j,\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p}},\textrm{ }\forall\mathfrak{z}\in D\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)

where
\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)
is the
completion of
\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}
with respect to the absolute
value
\mathcal{F}/I
associates to
\mathfrak{z}
.

II. For any
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
, the function
X_{\mathbf{n}}=\prod_{j=1}^{d}X_{j}^{n_{j}}\in C\left(\mathcal{F}/I\right)

is
\mathcal{F}/I
-quasi-integrable. Moreover, we denote a Fourier
transform of this function by:

(4.194)

\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}=\bigstar_{j=1}^{d}\hat{X}_{j}^{*n_{j}}\left(t\right)

where
\bigstar
denotes formal convolution, with
\hat{X}_{j}^{*n_{j}}\left(t\right)

being defined as the convolution identity element
\mathbf{1}_{0}

whenever
n_{j}=0
. Here,
\bigstar_{j=1}^{d}\hat{X}_{j}^{*n_{j}}

is a function
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\rightarrow\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}\right)\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)
.

Using
Theorem
10
to compute
\hat{X}_{j}

for all
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
, we then have the following
formula for
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}
:

(4.195)

\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)=\hat{f}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)-\hat{g}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)

where:

(4.196)

\hat{f}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\sum_{n=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-2}\left(\prod_{m=0}^{n-1}\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(p^{m}t\right)\right)\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(p^{n}t\right)\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(p^{n+1}t\right)

where the sum is taken over all
\mathbf{m}=\left(m_{1},\ldots,m_{d}\right)

so that
m_{j}\leq n_{j}
for all
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}

and
m_{j}<n_{j}
for at least one
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
,
and where:

(4.197)

\hat{g}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)=\begin{cases}0&\textrm{if }t=0\\
\left(\beta_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)v_{p}\left(t\right)+\gamma_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\frac{t\left|t\right|_{p}}{p}\right)\right)\hat{A}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)&\textrm{if }t\neq 0\end{cases},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

if
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1
and:

(4.198)

\hat{g}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)=\begin{cases}\frac{\beta_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}&\textrm{if }t=0\\
\left(\frac{\beta_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}+\gamma_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\frac{t\left|t\right|_{p}}{p}\right)\right)\hat{A}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)&\textrm{if }t\neq 0\end{cases},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

if
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\neq 1
, where, for all
\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}

with
m_{j}\leq n_{j}
for all
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
,

\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}},\beta_{\mathbf{n}},\gamma_{\mathbf{n}},\hat{A}_{\mathbf{n}}:\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\rightarrow\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}\right)\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)

are given by:

(4.199)

\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}e^{-2\pi ikt}

(4.200)

\beta_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}c_{\mathbf{n},k}e^{-2\pi ikt}

(4.201)

\gamma_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\frac{\beta_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)}{\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)}

(4.202)

\hat{A}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\prod_{n=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1}\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(p^{n}t\right)

where:

(4.203)

c_{\mathbf{n},k}=-\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},k}\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)

(4.204)

r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\binom{\mathbf{n}}{\mathbf{m}}a_{\mathbf{m},k}b_{\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{m},k}

(4.205)

r_{\mathbf{n},k}\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}r_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{n},k}

(4.206)

a_{\mathbf{m},k}\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\prod_{j=1}^{d}a_{j,k}^{m_{j}}

(4.207)

b_{\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{m},k}\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\prod_{j=1}^{d}b_{j,k}^{n_{j}-m_{j}}

(4.208)

\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\alpha_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)

III. For all
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
,
\hat{X}_{j}

will be the Fourier-Stieltjes transform of a distribution in
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}\right)\right)^{\prime}
,
which we identify with the symbol
X_{j}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}
.
Then, for any
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
, we define a pointwise
product:

(4.209)

X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\left(X_{j}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}\right)^{n_{j}}\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{d}X_{j}^{n_{j}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)d\mathfrak{z}

where
\left(\prod_{j=1}^{d}X_{j}^{n_{j}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)d\mathfrak{z}

is the distribution whose Fourier-Stieltjes transform is
\bigstar_{j=1}^{d}\hat{X}_{j}^{*n_{j}}
,
as given by (II). Consequently, we get a polynomial algebra
K\left[X_{1}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z},\ldots,X_{d}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}\right]
.
Moreover, the Fourier transform induces an isomorphism from this algebra
to the
K
-algebra generated by
\hat{X}_{1},\ldots,\hat{X}_{d}

under convolution.

Proof: As stated earlier, the proof is by using
Theorem
10

as the base case and then applying
Theorem
9

for
\mathcal{R}_{d}
. This then justifies
Assumption
4
,
which shows that the computations from
Theorem
3

hold true.

Q.E.D.

Remark 38
.

The restriction of
D\left(\mathcal{F}\right)
to a subset of
\textrm{NiceDig}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)

is
probably
not necessary, but I have not investigated it in
detail, as the proofs here are already intricate enough. The main
point of sensitivity is
Proposition
24

and the way it interacts with
Propositions
26

and
27
.

While the
X_{\mathbf{n}}
s will always be distributions, the places
of
\mathcal{R}
(and, in the quotient case,
\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}
)
for which
X_{\mathbf{n}}
becomes a measure captures information
about the norms of
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}
and the
\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}
s
with respect to the supremum norm induced by the chosen place of
\mathcal{R}
.

Remark 39
.

For what follows, given an ideal
I\subseteq R
, we write
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{p,\ell}

to denote the norm:

(4.210)

\left\|\hat{f}\right\|_{p,\ell}\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\sup_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\left|\hat{f}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}

where
\ell
is a place of
\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}
and
\left|\cdot\right|_{\ell}

is any associated absolute value;
\hat{f}
here is a function from

\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\rightarrow\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}\right)\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)
.

Corollary 4
.

Fix integers
p\geq 2
and
d\geq 1
,
and a unique solution ideal
I\subseteq R_{d}
. Using
Theorem

1
, fix a choice of Fourier transforms
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}

of
X_{\mathbf{n}}
for all
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
, and
use these to define
\hat{f}_{\mathbf{n}}
and
\hat{g}_{\mathbf{n}}

for all
\mathbf{n}
with
\Sigma\left(\mathbf{n}\right)\geq 2
.
Let
\ell
be a non-trivial place of
\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}
;
if
\ell
is non-archimedean, we require the residue field of the
completion
\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\ell}

to have characteristic co-prime to
p
. Finally, let
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}
.

I. If
\ell
is non-archimedean and
\max_{\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\leq 1
,
then the formula:
