\displaystyle W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\ell}}}\right)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\overline{\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\ell}}}

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{\phi}\left(t\right)\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(-t\right)

defines an element of
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\ell}}}\right)^{\prime}
.
This shows that
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}
is then the Fourier-Stieltjes
transform of
\left(p,\ell\right)
-adic measure, and as such, we
may identify this measure with
X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}

and write:

(4.211)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\phi\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{\phi}\left(t\right)\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(-t\right),\textrm{ }\forall\phi\in W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\ell}}}\right)

Moreover, the operator norm of this measure is:

(4.212)

\left\|X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}\right\|=\left\|\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\leq 1

II. If
\ell
is archimedean and:

(4.213)

\max_{\mathbf{m}:\mathbf{0}<\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}\left\{\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right\}<1

the Parseval-Plancherel construction given in (I) makes
X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}

into an element of
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\ell}}}\right)^{\prime}

if and only if
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\neq 1
in
\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}
.
When
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1
in
\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}
,

X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}
will merely
be a distribution in
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)^{\prime}
.

Proof: For brevity, let
\mathbb{K}
denote
\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}/I\mathcal{R}\right)
.
By construction, the continuous dual of
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{\ell}}}\right)

is then isometrically isomorphic to
B\left(\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{\ell}}}\right)

(see any of
[
18
,
16
,
13
,
11
]

for details), the Banach space of functions
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\rightarrow B\left(\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{\ell}}}\right)

with:

(4.214)

\lim_{\left|t\right|_{p}\rightarrow\infty}\left|\hat{f}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\overset{\mathbb{R}}{=}0

Thus, to show that
X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}

is an element of
W\left(\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{\ell}}}\right)^{\prime}
,
it suffices to show that that
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}
is
\ell
-adically
bounded over
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. We do this by induction on
\Sigma\left(\mathbf{n}\right)
.

For the base case, letting
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
,
Theorem

8
gives us:

(4.215)

\hat{X}_{j}\left(t\right)=\begin{cases}0&\textrm{if }t=0\\
\left(\beta_{j}\left(0\right)v_{p}\left(t\right)+\gamma_{j}\left(\frac{t\left|t\right|_{p}}{p}\right)\right)\hat{A}_{j}\left(t\right)&\textrm{if }t\neq 0\end{cases},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

when
\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)=1
, and:

(4.216)

\hat{X}_{j}\left(t\right)=\begin{cases}\frac{\beta_{j}\left(0\right)}{1-\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)}&\textrm{if }t=0\\
\frac{\beta_{j}\left(0\right)\hat{A}_{j}\left(t\right)}{1-\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)}+\gamma_{j}\left(\frac{t\left|t\right|_{p}}{p}\right)\hat{A}_{j}\left(t\right)&\textrm{if }t\neq 0\end{cases},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

when
\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)\neq 1
as Fourier transforms of
X_{j}
.

Here:

(4.217)

\hat{A}_{j}\left(t\right)=\prod_{n=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1}\alpha_{j}\left(p^{n}t\right)=\prod_{n=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1}\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}a_{j,k}e^{-2\pi ikp^{n}t}\right)

and:

(4.218)

\gamma_{j}\left(t\right)=\frac{\beta_{j}\left(t\right)}{\alpha_{j}\left(t\right)}=\frac{\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}b_{j,k}e^{-2\pi ikt}}{\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}a_{j,k}e^{-2\pi ikt}}

Here,
t\left|t\right|_{p}/p
is a rational number of the form
k/p

for all
t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, for some
k\in\left\{0,\ldots,p-1\right\}
,
with
k=0
if and only if
t=0
. Hence, for each
t
:

(4.219)

\gamma_{j}\left(\frac{t\left|t\right|_{p}}{p}\right)\in\mathbb{K}\left(e^{2\pi it}\right)

Likewise
\hat{A}_{j}\left(t\right)\in\mathbb{K}\left(e^{2\pi it}\right)
.
Thus, for each
t
, the quantity
\hat{X}_{j}\left(t\right)
is
contained in the cyclotomic extension
\mathbb{K}\left(e^{2\pi it}\right)

of
\mathbb{K}
.

So, letting
\ell
be a place of
\mathbb{K}
, have that
\left|\cdot\right|_{\ell}

admits a unique extension to
\mathbb{K}\left(e^{2\pi it}\right)

for each
t
, and so we may consider the extension of
\left|\cdot\right|_{\ell}

to the maximal
p
-power cyclotomic extension of
\mathbb{K}
.

Since
\gamma_{j}\left(t\left|t\right|_{p}/p\right)
takes on only
finitely many values as
t
varies, our formula for
\hat{X}_{j}\left(t\right)

gives:

(4.220)

\left|\hat{X}_{j}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\ll\begin{cases}\left|\min\left\{0,v_{p}\left(t\right)\right\}\right|_{\ell}\left|\hat{A}_{j}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}&\textrm{if }R_{j}\left(0\right)=1\\
\left|\hat{A}_{j}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}&\textrm{if }R_{j}\left(0\right)\neq 1\end{cases}

where
v_{p}\left(0\right)=+\infty
and
v_{p}\left(t\right)<0

for all
t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
. Every

\ell
-adically bounded function
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\rightarrow\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{\ell}}}

induces a measure on
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{\ell}}}\right)

by the Parseval-Plancherel construction given in
Theorem
2

(see
[
18
]
for details). Hence, letting:

(4.221)

\textrm{m}_{p,j}\left(t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\begin{cases}\min\left\{0,v_{p}\left(t\right)\right\}&\textrm{if }R_{j}\left(0\right)=1\\
1&\textrm{if }R_{j}\left(0\right)\neq 1\end{cases}

we have:

(4.222)

\sup_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\left|\textrm{m}_{p,j}\left(t\right)\hat{A}_{j}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}<\infty

implies
X_{j}
is a measure in
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{\ell}}}\right)^{\prime}

via the aforementioned the Parseval-Plancherel construction. Now,
we have two cases, based on the quality of
\ell
.

I. Suppose
\ell
is non-archimedean, and that:

(4.223)

\max_{\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\leq 1

Here, the non-archimedean quality of
\ell
gives us
\left|\textrm{m}_{p,j}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\leq 1

for all
j
. So, for any
\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}
with
\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}
:

(4.224)

\left|\hat{A}_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}=\prod_{n=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1}\left|\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(p^{n}t\right)\right|_{\ell}\leq\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{\max\left\{0,-v_{p}\left(t\right)\right\}},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

as such, using
Theorem
1
’s formula for

\hat{X}_{j}
for any given
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
, we
have:

(4.225)

\left|\hat{X}_{j}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\ll_{j,\ell}\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{\max\left\{0,-v_{p}\left(t\right)\right\}},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

as well as the trivial bound on
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{0}}=\mathbf{1}_{0}
,
where, as indicated, the constant of proportionality depends only
on
j
and
\ell
. Thus,
\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\leq 1

is sufficient to prove that
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)

is the Fourier transform of a
\left(p,\ell\right)
-adic measure
in the base case where
\Sigma\left(\mathbf{n}\right)=1
.

Moving to the inductive step, suppose that
\max_{\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\leq 1

and:

(4.226)

\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\left\|\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\right\|_{p,\ell}<\infty

Using:

(4.227)

\hat{f}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\sum_{n=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-2}\left(\prod_{m=0}^{n-1}\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(p^{m}t\right)\right)\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(p^{n}t\right)\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(p^{n+1}t\right)

we apply the
\ell
-adic ultrametric inequality to get:

(4.228)

\left|\hat{f}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\leq\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\max_{0\leq n\leq-v_{p}\left(t\right)-2}\left|\left(\prod_{m=0}^{n-1}\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(p^{m}t\right)\right)\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(p^{n}t\right)\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(p^{n+1}t\right)\right|_{\ell}

Meanwhile, the formula for
\hat{g}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)
and
the
\ell
-adic boundedness of
v_{p}\left(t\right)
on
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\backslash\left\{0\right\}

gives:

(4.229)

\left|\hat{g}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\ll_{\mathbf{n},\ell}\left|\hat{A}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

where, as indicated, the constant of proportionality depends only
on
\mathbf{n}
and
\ell
. Then:

\displaystyle\left|\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\left|\hat{g}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell},\left|\hat{f}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\right\}

\displaystyle\ll_{\mathbf{n},\ell}\max\left\{\left|\hat{A}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell},\max_{I\subset\mathbf{n}}\max_{0\leq n\leq-v_{p}\left(t\right)-2}\left|\left(\prod_{m=0}^{n-1}\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(p^{m}t\right)\right)\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(p^{n}t\right)\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(p^{n+1}t\right)\right|_{\ell}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\left|\hat{A}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell},\underbrace{\left(\max_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\left\|\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right)}_{<\infty}\underbrace{\left(\max_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}<\mathbf{n}}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right)}_{\infty}\left(\max\left\{1,\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right\}\right)^{\max\left\{0,-v_{p}\left(t\right)-2\right\}}\right\}

\displaystyle\ll_{\mathbf{n},\ell}\max\left\{\left|\hat{A}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell},\left(\max\left\{1,\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right\}\right)^{\max\left\{0,-v_{p}\left(t\right)-2\right\}}\right\}

Here, note that
\max_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}<\mathbf{n}}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}<\infty

independent of any hypothesis, since each
\alpha_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}}

is a degree
p-1
polynomial in
e^{-2\pi it}
. Meanwhile:

(4.230)

\left|\hat{A}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}=\left|\prod_{m=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1}\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(p^{m}t\right)\right|_{\ell}\leq\left(\max\left\{1,\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right\}\right)^{\max\left\{0,-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1\right\}}

and so:

(4.231)

\left|\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\ll_{\mathbf{n},\ell}\left(\max\left\{1,\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right\}\right)^{\max\left\{0,-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1\right\}}\leq 1,\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

Hence, the hypothesis
\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\leq 1

is then sufficient to show that
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}
is the Fourier-Stieltjes
transform of a
\left(p,\ell\right)
-adic measure,
X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}
.
Since the Fourier-Stieltjes transform is an isometric isomorphism
from
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{\ell}}}\right)^{\prime}

onto the space of bounded functions
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\rightarrow\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{\ell}}}
,
we then get the estimate:

(4.232)

\left\|X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}\right\|=\left\|\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\leq 1

as desired.

II. Suppose
\ell
is archimedean. Like before, we get the estimate:

(4.233)

\left|\hat{A}_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\leq\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{\max\left\{0,-v_{p}\left(t\right)\right\}},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\neq 1
, we do not need to contend
with a factor of
v_{p}\left(t\right)
in
\hat{g}_{\mathbf{n}}
,
and so we once again get the estimate:

(4.234)

\left|\hat{g}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\ll_{\mathbf{n},\ell}\left|\hat{A}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\leq\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{\max\left\{0,-v_{p}\left(t\right)\right\}},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

As for
\hat{f}_{\mathbf{n}}
, using the ordinary triangle inequality
gives:

(4.235)

\left|\hat{f}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\ll_{\mathbf{n}}\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\sum_{n=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-2}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{n}\left|\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(p^{n+1}t\right)\right|_{\ell}

Since
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{0}}=\mathbf{1}_{0}
,
\mathbf{m}=\mathbf{0}

term of the upper bound is bounded by:

(4.236)

\sum_{n=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-2}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{n}\leq\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{n},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

Hence, our assumption that
\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}<1

guarantees the bound on the
\mathbf{m}=\mathbf{0}
th term. As such,
it suffices to deal with the terms for which
\mathbf{m}>\mathbf{0}
.

Now, for
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
, we have:

(4.237)

\left|\hat{X}_{j}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\ll_{j,\ell}\left(-v_{p}\left(t\right)\right)^{\left[\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)=1\right]}\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{\max\left\{0,-v_{p}\left(t\right)\right\}},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

Thus, for
\Sigma\left(\mathbf{n}\right)=2
, letting
\mathbf{e}_{j}

denote the element of
\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with a
1
in the
j
the
entry and a
0
in all other entries, we have:

\displaystyle\left|\hat{f}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}

\displaystyle\ll_{\mathbf{n}}\sum_{j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}:n_{j}\geq 1}\sum_{n=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-2}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{n}\left|\hat{X}_{j}\left(p^{n+1}t\right)\right|_{\ell}

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}:n_{j}\geq 1}\sum_{n=0}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-2}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{n}\left(-v_{p}\left(t\right)-n-1\right)^{\left[\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)=1\right]}\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{\max\left\{0,-v_{p}\left(t\right)-n-1\right\}}

\displaystyle\left(m=-v_{p}\left(t\right)-n\right);

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}:n_{j}\geq 1}\sum_{m=1}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-m-1}m^{\left[\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)=1\right]}\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{m+1}

(4.238)

\displaystyle\ll_{\mathbf{n}}\sum_{j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}:n_{j}\geq 1}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)}\sum_{m=1}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1}m^{\left[\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)=1\right]}\left(\frac{\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell}}{\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}}\right)^{m}

Here, we get several possibilities:

•
\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)\neq 1
, in which case:

(4.239)

\sum_{m=1}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1}m^{\left[\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)=1\right]}\left(\frac{\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell}}{\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}}\right)^{m}\ll 1+\left(\frac{\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell}}{\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}}\right)^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)}

•
\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)=1
and
\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell}/\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\neq 1
,
in which case:

(4.240)

\sum_{m=1}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1}m^{\left[\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)=1\right]}\left(\frac{\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell}}{\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}}\right)^{m}\ll 1+\left|v_{p}\left(t\right)\right|\left(\frac{\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell}}{\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}}\right)^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)}

•
\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)=1
and
\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell}/\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}=1
,
in which case:

(4.241)

\sum_{m=1}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)-1}m^{\left[\alpha_{j}\left(0\right)=1\right]}\left(\frac{\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell}}{\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}}\right)^{m}\ll\left|v_{p}\left(t\right)\right|^{2}

Using this with (
4.238
), we see that
\left|\hat{f}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}

will remain bounded as
\left|t\right|_{p}\rightarrow\infty
provided
that:

(4.242)

\max\left\{\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell},\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right\}<1,\textrm{ }\forall j:\mathbf{e}_{j}\leq\mathbf{n}

In particular, if this condition is satisfied, we will have:

(4.243)

\left|\hat{f}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\ll_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\max\left\{1,\max_{j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}:n_{j}\geq 1}\left\{\left\|\alpha_{j}\right\|_{p,\ell},\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{e}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right\}\right\}\right)^{\max\left\{0,-v_{p}\left(t\right)\right\}}

By induction on
\left|\mathbf{n}\right|
, we have that:

(4.244)

\left|\hat{f}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\ll_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\max\left\{1,\max_{\mathbf{m}:\mathbf{0}<\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}\left\{\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m}}\right\|_{p,\ell},\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right\}\right\}\right)^{\max\left\{0,-v_{p}\left(t\right)\right\}}

will occur provided that:

(4.245)

\max_{\mathbf{m}:\mathbf{0}<\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}\max\left\{\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m}}\right\|_{p,\ell},\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right\}<1

Meanwhile, the formula for
\hat{g}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)
gives:

(4.246)

\left|\hat{g}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\ll_{\mathbf{n},\ell}\left|v_{p}\left(t\right)\right|^{\left[\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1\right]}\left|\hat{A}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\right|_{\ell}\leq\left|v_{p}\left(t\right)\right|^{\left[\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1\right]}\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}^{-v_{p}\left(t\right)},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\backslash\left\{0\right\}

Hence, as hypothesized:

(4.247)

\max_{\mathbf{m}:\mathbf{0}<\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}\max\left\{\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m}}\right\|_{p,\ell},\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right\}<1

is then sufficient to guarantee that both
\hat{f}_{\mathbf{n}}

and
\hat{g}_{\mathbf{n}}
are bounded with respect to
\left|\cdot\right|_{\ell}
,
and thus, that
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}
is the Fourier-Stieltjes transform
of an element of
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{\ell}}}\right)^{\prime}
.

Finally, note that if there is an
\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}
with

\Sigma\left(\mathbf{m}\right)\geq 1
so that either
\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1

or
\alpha_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)=1
, it will follow that
\max_{\mathbf{m}:\mathbf{0}<\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}\max\left\{\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m}}\right\|_{p,\ell},\left\|\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,\ell}\right\}=1
,
which contradicts the hypothesis (
4.213
). In
this case,
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}
will no longer be bounded with
respect to
t
, and thus will
not
be the Fourier-Stieltjes
transform of an element of
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{\ell}}}\right)^{\prime}
.

Q.E.D.

Remark 40
.

One can do fun things with this result. Keeping the notation as from
the above theorem, suppose
\ell=q
is non-archimedean, and that
we are working in the power case, with
X^{n}
being a
\left(p,q\right)
-adic
measure for all
n\geq 0
. For simplicity, let’s suppose that we can
realize
X
as a
q
-adic valued function on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
An concrete example of this would be:

(4.248)

X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{0}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{p-1}a_{j}^{\#_{p:j}\left(\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n}}\right)}

where the
a_{j}
s are elements of
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
so that

\left|a_{j}\right|_{q}<1
for all
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,p-1\right\}
.
This F-series is then compatible with respect to the frame which assigns
the real topology to
\mathfrak{z}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and assigns
the
q
-adic topology to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\backslash\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

Next, letting
\mathbb{C}_{q}
be the metric completion of the algebraic
closure of
\mathbb{Q}_{q}
, let:

(4.249)

U_{q}\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\left\{\mathfrak{z}\in\mathbb{C}_{q}:\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathfrak{z}^{n}}{n!}\textrm{ converges in }\mathbb{C}_{q}\right\}

so that
U_{q}
is the disk of convergence of the
q
-adic exponential
function
\exp_{q}:U_{q}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{q}
.

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
X^{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}

is a
\left(p,q\right)
-adic measure, with:

(4.250)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\phi\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)X^{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}=\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{\phi}\left(t\right)\hat{X}^{*n}\left(-t\right)

for all continuous
\phi:\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{q}
.
Here, the theorem shows that:

(4.251)

\left|\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\phi\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)X^{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}\right|_{q}\leq\left\|\phi\right\|_{p,q}\underbrace{\left\|\hat{X}^{*n}\right\|_{p,q}}_{\leq 1}\leq\left\|\phi\right\|_{p,q}

As such, letting
\mathfrak{y}\in U_{q}
, the expression:

(4.252)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\phi\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathfrak{y}^{n}}{n!}X^{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}

converges absolutely in
\mathbb{C}_{q}
, and therefore defines
\left(p,q\right)
-adic
measure:

(4.253)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\phi\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\exp_{q}\left(\mathfrak{y}X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)d\mathfrak{z}\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{\phi}\left(t\right)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\hat{X}^{*n}\left(-t\right)}{n!}\mathfrak{y}^{n}

In this way, the map:

(4.254)

\displaystyle U_{q}

\displaystyle\rightarrow W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{C}_{q}\right)^{\prime}

(4.255)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{y}

\displaystyle\mapsto\exp_{q}\left(\mathfrak{y}X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)d\mathfrak{z}

is then analytic, and, for fixed
\phi\in W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{C}_{q}\right)
,
(
4.253
) is then an analytic function

U_{q}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{q}
.
\exp_{q}\left(\mathfrak{y}X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)d\mathfrak{z}

is the
moment generating measure
of
X
.

Similarly, in the archimedean case, if we can realize
X
as a uniformly
continuous function
X:\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, we then
have that:

(4.256)

\phi_{X}\left(s\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{sX\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}d\mathfrak{z}

is an entire function of
s
, and is the moment generating function
of
X
, because:

(4.257)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{sX\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}d\mathfrak{z}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}X^{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}\right)\frac{s^{n}}{n!}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\hat{X}^{*n}\left(0\right)\frac{s^{n}}{n!}

If
X
is merely in
L^{1}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{C}\right)

(absolutely integrable with respect to the real-valued Haar probability
measure), the moment generating function need no longer be entire.
For a concrete example, consider:

(4.258)

\displaystyle X\left(2\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}{1+i}

(4.259)

\displaystyle X\left(2\mathfrak{z}+1\right)

\displaystyle=X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)+i

Though the restriction of
X
to
\mathbb{N}_{0}
is well-defined,
the extension of
X
to
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is convergent only for
those
\mathfrak{z}
whose
1
s digits do not have density
1

among
\mathfrak{z}
’s
2
-adic digits. Here,
\phi_{X}
satisfies:

(4.260)

\phi_{X}\left(s\right)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}e^{\frac{s}{1+i}X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}d\mathfrak{z}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}e^{s\left(X\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)+i\right)}d\mathfrak{z}=\phi_{X}\left(\frac{s}{1+i}\right)+\frac{e^{si}}{2}\phi_{X}\left(s\right)

from which we obtain:

(4.261)

\phi_{X}\left(s\right)=\phi_{X}\left(\frac{s}{\left(1+i\right)^{N}}\right)\prod_{n=0}^{N-1}\left(2-e^{\frac{is}{\left(1+i\right)^{n}}}\right)^{-1},\textrm{ }\forall N\geq 1

It is easy to show that the limit of the partial product as
N\rightarrow\infty

converges uniformly on all compact subsets of
\mathbb{C}
avoiding
the points:

(4.262)

s=\left(1+i\right)^{n}\left(2\pi k-i\ln 2\right),\textrm{ }\forall n\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\textrm{ }\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}

Using the fact that
\phi_{X}\left(0\right)=1
, it follows that:

(4.263)

\phi_{X}\left(s\right)=\prod_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(2-e^{\frac{is}{\left(1+i\right)^{n}}}\right)^{-1}

is meromorphic on
\mathbb{C}
, as its reciprocal is an entire function.
The poles are all simple, and are located at (
4.262
).
As
-i\ln 2
is the pole closest to the origin, we then have the asymptotic
equivalence:

\hat{X}^{*n}\left(0\right)\sim C_{0}\left(i\ln 2\right)^{n}

for some complex constant
C_{0}
. More generally:

(4.264)

\phi_{X}\left(s,t\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{sX\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)}e^{-2\pi i\left\{t\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}d\mathfrak{z},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

is the exponential generating function whose
n
th coefficient is

\hat{X}^{*n}\left(t\right)
:

(4.265)

\phi_{X}\left(s,t\right)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\hat{X}^{*n}\left(t\right)\frac{s^{n}}{n!}

5.
Measures, Varieties, & Arithmetic Dynamics

Remark 41
.

For everything we do in this section, we can replace each instance
of
\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)

with
\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)
,
where
I\subseteq R_{d}
is any unique solution ideal. As such, we
will omit reference to
I
, except when stating major results.

Section
5.1
establishes the paper’s
third main result,
Theorem
12
on
page
12
, thereby furnishing a more comprehensive
resolution of
Questions
1

and

2
from page
1

of
Section
2.2
.

Section
5.2
uses the
work of
Section
5.1
to illuminate
the ways in which F-series and degenerate measures can be used to
encode information about algebraic varieties. Finally,
Section

5.3
gives some examples
and conjectural explorations for future research in using F-series
to better understand certain types of arithmetic dynamical systems.

5.1.
Formal Solutions & Breakdown Varieties

In
Example
2
from page
2

of
Section
2.2
, we treated

X_{\mathbf{n}}
as if it was integrable and assumed that it satisfied
the natural transformation laws under changes of variables induced
by its functional equations. Under those assumptions, we had the following:

\displaystyle\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{-2\pi i\left\{t\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p}{\equiv}k\right]e^{-2\pi i\left\{t\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\int_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}+k}e^{-2\pi i\left\{t\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}X_{n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}

\displaystyle\left(\textrm{change of variable}\right);

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{-2\pi i\left\{t\left(p\mathfrak{z}+k\right)\right\}_{p}}X_{\mathbf{n}}\left(p\mathfrak{z}+k\right)d\mathfrak{z}

\displaystyle\left(\textrm{apply }(\ref{eq:Z functional equation})\right);

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{-2\pi i\left\{pt\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}e^{-2\pi ikt}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}X_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right)d\mathfrak{z}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{e}}\underbrace{\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}e^{-2\pi ikt}\right)}_{\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)}\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{-2\pi i\left\{pt\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}X_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}}_{\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(pt\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(pt\right)

We showed in
Proposition
6

that the functional equation:

(5.1)

\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(pt\right)

could be used to recursively solve for
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}
uniquely
provided
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\neq 1
.

By using truncations (precomposing
X_{\mathbf{n}}
with the projection
map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
, we saw
a second way to obtain this functional equation, namely, as the formal
limit as
N\rightarrow\infty
of the functional equations satisfied
by the Fourier transform of
X_{\mathbf{n},N}
, the
N
th truncation
of
X_{\mathbf{n}}
:

(5.2)

\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n},N}\left(t\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m},N-1}\left(pt\right)

If the Fourier transforms of the
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}
s exist in
the classical sense—say, there is a metrically complete,
algebraically closed valued field
\mathbb{K}
with characteristic
either co-prime to
p
(if the characteristic is positive) and/or
with a residue field of characteristic co-prime to
p
(if
\mathbb{K}

is non-archimedean) so that
X_{\mathbf{m}}\in W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{K}\right)

for all
\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}
—then the limit
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m},N}\left(t\right)

converges in
\mathbb{K}
to
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(t\right)
,
the Fourier integral of
X_{\mathbf{m}}
:

(5.3)

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m},N}\left(t\right)\overset{\mathbb{K}}{=}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}X_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)e^{-2\pi i\left\{t\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}d\mathfrak{z},\textrm{ }\forall\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}

where
d\mathfrak{z}
is the
\mathbb{K}
-valued Haar probability
measure on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Here, the existence of the integral
follows by classical abstract harmonic analysis and Pontryagin duality.
Moreover, since
X_{\mathbf{m}}
is in the Wiener algebra, the Fourier
series generated by
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}
will sum to
X_{\mathbf{m}}

for all
\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}
.

Thus, in this case, provided
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\neq 1

and all the
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{m}}
s are already known, the equation
(
5.1
) can be solved recursively to obtain
a closed-form expression for the Fourier transform of
X_{\mathbf{n}}
.
This is much nicer than the methods we’ve used so far, because it
involves no guesswork and can be done completely systematically. The
purpose of this subsection is to show that, even when
X_{\mathbf{n}}

is merely quasi-integrable, so long as
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\neq 1
,
this same formal method of using (
5.1
)
to compute
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}
does, in fact, “work”, in the
sense that the answer obtained is indeed a Fourier transform of
\hat{X}_{\mathbf{n}}

with respect to an appropriately chosen frame.

We begin with some definitions.

Definition 36
.

Viewing
\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
as a partially ordered set with respect
to the relations
\leq
and
<
, given
\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}

with
\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}
, we write
\left[\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}\right]

to denote the set of all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
so that
\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{k}\leq\mathbf{n}

and call
\left[\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}\right]
the
closed
interval from
\mathbf{m}
to
n
. We similarly co-opt standard
interval notation to denote open intervals and half-open intervals
in the obvious way (Ex:
\left[\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}\right)
is the
set of all
\mathbf{k}
for which
\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{k}<\mathbf{n}
).
We say an interval
I\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
is
bounded

whenever
\sup_{\mathbf{k}\in I}\Sigma\left(\mathbf{k}\right)<\infty
.

Let
\textrm{Map}\left(\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)\right)

denote the space of all functions
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)
.
Given an
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
, the
\mathbf{n}
th
formal equation
/
\mathbf{n}
th functional equation
is:

(5.4)

\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\left[\mathbf{0},\mathbf{n}\right]\cap I}\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(pt\right)

for undetermined functions
\left\{\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}_{\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}}

in
\textrm{Map}\left(\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)\right)
.
Given a bounded interval
I\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
, the
I
th
formal system /
I
th functional equation
is the system of equations:

(5.5)

\left\{\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\left[\mathbf{0},\mathbf{n}\right]\cap I}\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(pt\right),\textrm{ }\forall\mathbf{n}\in I\right\}

for undetermined functions
\left\{\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}_{\mathbf{n}\in I}

in
\textrm{Map}\left(\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)\right)
.
Note that for any
\mathbf{n}
, the
\left[\mathbf{n},\mathbf{n}\right]
th
formal system is then precisely the
\mathbf{n}
th formal equation.

Given an interval
I
, an
I
th formal solution
is a collection
of functions
\left\{\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}_{\mathbf{n}\in I}

in
\textrm{Map}\left(\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)\right)

so that setting
\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}=\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{n}}
for
all
\mathbf{n}\in I
makes (
5.5
) hold true
for all
t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.

Given an interval
J\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
, a
J
th
initial condition
is a collection of functions
\left\{\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}_{\mathbf{n}\in J}

in
\textrm{Map}\left(\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)\right)
.
The
I
th formal system (
a.k.a.
I
th functional
equation) relative to an initial condition
\left\{\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}_{\mathbf{n}\in J}

is:

(5.6)

\left\{\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\left[\mathbf{0},\mathbf{n}\right]\cap\left(I\backslash J\right)}\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(pt\right)+\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\left[\mathbf{0},\mathbf{n}\right]\cap J}\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(pt\right),\textrm{ }\forall\mathbf{n}\in I\right\}

for undetermined functions
\left\{\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}_{\mathbf{n}\in I\backslash J}

in
\textrm{Map}\left(\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)\right)
.
An
I
th formal solution relative to an initial condition

\left\{\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}_{\mathbf{n}\in J}

is a collection of functions
\left\{\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{n}}\right\}_{\mathbf{n}\in I\backslash J}

in
\textrm{Map}\left(\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)\right)

so that
\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}=\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{n}}
for all
\mathbf{n}\in I\backslash J

satisfies (
5.6
) for all
t
.

For all of the notions presented so far, given a unique solution ideal

I\subseteq R_{d}
, we will add the qualifier
mod
I

to
indicate that the equalities occur in
\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)
.

Now, as we observed in
Proposition
6

from
Section
2.2
, given
any
\mathbf{n}>\mathbf{0}
and any initial condition
\left\{\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}_{\mathbf{m}\in\left[\mathbf{0},\mathbf{n}\right)}
,
the
\mathbf{n}
th functional equation relative to this initial condition
has a unique solution if and only if
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\neq 1
.
When
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1
, the equation breaks down,
and we have one of two possibilities: either:

(5.7)

\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(0\right)=0

in which case there are infinitely many
\mathbf{n}
th solutions

\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)
, one for each initial value

\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\in\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)
,
or the above equality fails to hold, in which case there is no
\mathbf{n}
th
solution.

This motivates the following definition:

Definition 37
.

Fix
d\geq 1
and a unique solution ideal
I\subseteq R_{d}
. Given
any
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}
,
we write
\textrm{Break}_{I}\left(\mathbf{n}\right)
to denote the
locus of
a_{j,k}
s and
b_{j,k}
s in
K
so that
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=1

holds true in
\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}
. We call
\textrm{Break}_{I}\left(\mathbf{n}\right)

the
\mathbf{n}
th breakdown variety relative to
I
. We
also call
\textrm{Break}_{I}\left(\mathbf{n}\right)
the
breakdown
variety of
X_{\mathbf{n}}
relative to
I
,

and write
it as
\textrm{Break}_{I}\left(X_{\mathbf{n}}\right)

We then write
\textrm{CoBreak}_{I}\left(\mathbf{n}\right)
to denote
the locus of all
a_{j,k}
s and
b_{j,k}
s in
K
so that the equation

\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\neq 1
holds true in
\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}
.
We call
\textrm{CoBreak}_{I}\left(\mathbf{n}\right)
the
\mathbf{n}
th
co-breakdown variety relative to
I
. We also call
\textrm{CoBreak}_{I}\left(\mathbf{n}\right)

the
co-breakdown variety of
X_{\mathbf{n}}
relative to

I
, and write it as
\textrm{CoBreak}_{I}\left(X_{\mathbf{n}}\right)

Next, we write:

(5.8)

\textrm{BI}_{d}\left(I\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\left\{\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}:\left\langle 1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right\rangle\subseteq I\right\}

and:

(5.9)

\textrm{CBI}_{d}\left(I\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\left\{\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}:\left\langle 1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right\rangle\nsubseteq I\right\}

We call these the sets of
breakdown indices
and
co-breakdown
indices
associated to
I
.

Example 10
.

When
d=1
, the
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{1}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
th
breakdown variety relative to
\left\langle 0\right\rangle
(a.k.a.,
the
n
th breakdown variety of
X
relative to
0
) is the locus
of all
a_{0},\ldots,a_{p-1}\in K
so that:

(5.10)

\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}a_{k}^{n}=p

Thus, for example, the breakdown variety of
X^{2}
relative to
\left\langle 0\right\rangle

is the locus of points in
K
lying on the
\left(p-1\right)
-sphere
of radius
p
.

Definition 38
.

We
breakdown indices
\textrm{BI}_{d}\left(I\right)
as
the set of all
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\Sigma\left(\mathbf{n}\right)\geq 2

for which
\left\langle 1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right\rangle\subseteq I
.
Let
\textrm{CBI}_{d}\left(I\right)
, the
co-breakdown indices

be the set of all
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\Sigma\left(\mathbf{n}\right)\geq 2

for which
\left\langle 1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right\rangle\notin I
.
We
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
is
trivial
when
\Sigma\left(\mathbf{n}\right)\leq 1
.

Relative to an initial condition
\left\{\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}_{\mathbf{m}\in\left[\mathbf{0},\mathbf{n}\right)}
,
the
\mathbf{n}
th functional equation can be written as:

(5.11)

\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(pt\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(pt\right)

Letting
dY_{\mathbf{n}}
and
d\chi_{\mathbf{m}}
denote the distributions
with
\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}
and
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}
as their
Fourier transforms, we can use (
5.11
) to compute
a functional equation giving the action of
dY_{\mathbf{n}}
on an
SB function
\phi
in terms of the actions of the
d\chi_{\mathbf{m}}
s.

Proposition 33
.

Let
\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}

be any solution of the
\mathbf{n}
th functional equation relative
to an initial condition
\left\{\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}_{\mathbf{m}\in\left[0,\mathbf{n}\right)}
,
and let
dY_{\mathbf{n}}
and
d\chi_{\mathbf{m}}
be the distributions
on
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)\right)

with
\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}
and the
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}
s as
their Fourier-Stieltjes transforms. Then, for all
\phi\in\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)\right)
:

(5.12)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\phi\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{n},k}\phi\left(p\mathfrak{z}+k\right)\right)dY_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}\phi\left(p\mathfrak{z}+k\right)\right)d\chi_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

Proof: Multiplying (
5.11
) by
\hat{\phi}\left(-t\right)

and summing over all
t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, where
\phi\in\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)\right)
,
and using:

(5.13)

\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{\phi}\left(-t\right)\hat{\mu}\left(t\right)=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\phi\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mu\left(\mathfrak{z}\right),\textrm{ }\forall d\mu\in\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)\right)^{\prime}

we get:

(5.14)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\phi\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)dY_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)-\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{\phi}\left(-t\right)\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(pt\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{\phi}\left(-t\right)\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(pt\right)

Here, using the Fourier-Stieltjes transform of
dY_{\mathbf{n}}
,
we get:

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{\phi}\left(-t\right)\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}\left(pt\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{\phi}\left(-t\right)\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)e^{-2\pi i\left\{pt\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}\right)dY_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{n},k}e^{-2\pi ikt}\right);

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{n},k}\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{\phi}\left(-t\right)e^{2\pi i\left\{-t\left(p\mathfrak{z}+k\right)\right\}_{p}}\right)dY_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{n},k}\phi\left(p\mathfrak{z}+k\right)\right)dY_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

Likewise, for
d\chi_{\mathbf{m}}
:

(5.15)

\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\hat{\phi}\left(-t\right)\alpha_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left(t\right)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}\left(pt\right)=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}\phi\left(p\mathfrak{z}+k\right)\right)d\chi_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

Putting all this together, (
5.11
) becomes:

(5.16)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\phi\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{n},k}\phi\left(p\mathfrak{z}+k\right)\right)dY_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}\phi\left(p\mathfrak{z}+k\right)\right)d\chi_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

Q.E.D.

The formula that we got motivates the following definition:

Definition 39
.

For any
\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{n}
,
define
L_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}:\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)

by:

(5.17)

L_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left\{\phi\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}\phi\left(p\mathfrak{z}+k\right),\textrm{ }\forall\phi\in\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)

We then write
L_{\mathbf{n}}
to denote
L_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{n}}
.

The following is then obvious:

Proposition 34
.

L_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}
is a
linear operator. Moreover, letting
v
be any absolute value on
\mathcal{R}_{d}
,
and letting
\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{v}}}
be the metric
completion of the algebraic closure of the metric completion
\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)

with respect to
v
, we have that
L_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}
uniquely
extends to a
continuous
linear operator
L:W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{v}}}\right)\rightarrow W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{v}}}\right)
.
We have the operator norm of
L_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}
over
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\mathbb{K}_{v}}}\right)

satisfies the bound:

(5.18)

\left\|L_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\right\|_{p,v}\leq\begin{cases}\max_{0\leq k<p}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}}{p}\right|_{v}&\textrm{if }v<\infty\\
\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}}{p}\right|_{v}&\textrm{else}\end{cases}

where
v<\infty
means
v
is a non-archimedean place.

Proof: Triangle inequality in the archimedean case; ultrametric inequality
in the non-archimedean case.

Q.E.D.

With the
L_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}
s in hand, we can
write the result of
Proposition
33

as:

(5.19)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)\left\{\phi\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)dY_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}L_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\left\{\phi\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\chi_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

The left-hand side is the image of
\phi
under the composite
dY_{\mathbf{n}}\circ\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right):\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)\rightarrow\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)
.
As such, observe that if
1-L_{\mathbf{n}}:\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)

was
invertible
, by setting
\phi
equal to
\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{\psi\right\}
,
where
\psi\in\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)

was arbitrary, we would get:

(5.20)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\psi\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)dY_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\sum_{\mathbf{m}<\mathbf{n}}\left(L_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}\circ\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\right)\left\{\psi\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\chi_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right),\textrm{ }\forall\psi\in\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)

The right-hand side is then a closed-form expression of
dY_{\mathbf{n}}

in terms of the
d\chi_{\mathbf{m}}
s. In particular, we see that:

Proposition 35
.

The
\mathbf{n}
th formal solution
dY_{\mathbf{n}}

relative to the initial condition
\left\{\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}_{\mathbf{m}\in\left[0,\mathbf{n}\right)}

is uniquely determined by the initial condition whenever
1-L_{\mathbf{n}}

is invertible on
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)
.

Unsurprisingly, it turns out that the invertibility holds if and only
if
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\neq 1
. To show this, we first
compute a closed-form expression for the image of the indicator function

\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]
under
m
iterations
of
1-L_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}
.

Proposition 36
.

Let
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
,
and let
m,n\geq 0
, and
k\in\left\{0,\ldots,p^{n}-1\right\}
.
Then:

(5.21)

L_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\begin{cases}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{m}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{m}}\right)}{p^{m}}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-m}}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(k\right)\right]&\textrm{if }m<n\\
\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{n},j}\right)^{m-n}&\textrm{if }m\geq n\end{cases}

where

(5.22)

\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(n\right)=\prod_{j=1}^{p-1}\left(\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},j}}{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}\right)^{\#_{p:j}\left(n\right)}

Proof: For brevity, we will omit the subscript
\mathbf{n}
s. Using
the definition of
L
, we have:

(5.23)

L\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}r_{j}\left[p\mathfrak{z}+j\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]

By induction, we obtain

(5.24)

L\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\left(\prod_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{r_{\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ j}\left(k\right)\right]_{p}}}{p}\right)\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-m}}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(k\right)\right]

By
Proposition
36
,
we have:

(5.25)

\prod_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{r_{\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ j}\left(k\right)\right]_{p}}}{p}=\frac{1}{p^{m}}r_{0}^{m}\prod_{j=1}^{p-1}\left(r_{j}/r_{0}\right)^{\#_{p:j}\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{m}}\right)}=\frac{r_{0}^{m}\kappa\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{m}}\right)}{p^{m}}

Hence:

(5.26)

L^{m}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\left(\prod_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{r_{\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ j}\left(k\right)\right]_{p}}}{p}\right)\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-m}}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(k\right)\right]=\frac{r_{0}^{m}\kappa\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{m}}\right)}{p^{m}}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-m}}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(k\right)\right]

Now, note that when
m=n
, we have:

(5.27)

L^{n}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\frac{r_{0}^{n}\kappa\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{n}}\right)}{p^{n}}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{1}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(k\right)\right]

Since
\mathfrak{z}
and
\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(k\right)
are
both
p
-adic integers, the congruence
\mathfrak{z}\overset{1}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ n}\left(k\right)

is always true, and so:

\displaystyle L^{n}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{0}^{n}\kappa\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{n}}\right)}{p^{n}}

\displaystyle\left(0\leq k\leq p^{n}-1\right);

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{0}^{n}\kappa\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}

is a constant function. Also, for the constant function
1
, we note
that:

(5.28)

L\left\{1\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}r_{j}

and so:

(5.29)

L^{m}\left\{1\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}r_{j}\right)^{m},\textrm{ }\forall m\geq 1

Consequently, when
m\geq n
, we have:

\displaystyle L^{m}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=L^{m-n}\left\{L^{n}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=L^{m-n}\left\{\frac{r_{0}^{n}\kappa\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{0}^{n}\kappa\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}r_{j}\right)^{m-n}

Q.E.D.

Proving the invertibility of
L_{\mathbf{n}}
is by a
straightforward, but unusual, argument.

Lemma 4
.

Let
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}
,
and let
I
be a unique solution ideal in
R_{d}
. Then,
1-L_{\mathbf{n}}:\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)

is an invertible linear transformation if and only if
\left\langle 1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right\rangle\cap I=\left\{0\right\}
.
When the inverse exists, we have that:

(5.30)

\frac{1}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}+\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{m}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{m}}\right)}{p^{m}}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-m}}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(k\right)\right]

is the image of
\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]

under
\left(1-L\right)^{-1}
.

Moreover, letting
\overline{\overline{\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\mathfrak{q}}}}

be the metric completion of the algebraic closure of the metric completion
of
\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)
with
respect to an absolute value
\left|\cdot\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}

on
\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}
, it then follows that
1-L_{\mathbf{n}}

extends to be invertible on
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\mathfrak{q}}}}\right)

whenever:

I. If
\mathfrak{q}
is non-archimedean:

(5.31)

\max_{0\leq k<p}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}}{p}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}\leq 1

II. If
\mathfrak{q}
is archimedean:

(5.32)

\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}}{p}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}<1

Proof: Again, we drop the subscript
\mathbf{n}
s except where necessary
for clarity. As everyone knows, one can always write:

(5.33)

\left(1-L\right)^{-1}=\frac{1}{1-L}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}L^{n}

provided that there is a complete normed space
\mathcal{X}
so that
the series on the right converges in
\mathcal{B}\left(\mathcal{X}\right)
,
the Banach algebra of continuous linear operators
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
,
with operator composition as its multiplication map.

So, working in
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)
—
note
,
we ARE NOT invoking the quotient by
I
yet!—since
\mathcal{R}_{d}\left(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}\right)

is the maximal
p
-power cyclotomic extension of the localization
of
R_{d}
away from
\left\langle 1-a_{1,0},\ldots,1-a_{d,0}\right\rangle
,
where
R_{d}
is a ring of polynomials with coefficients in
\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
we have that:

(5.34)

\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{n},k}=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\prod_{j=1}^{d}a_{j,k}^{n_{j}}

is
not
a unit of
\mathcal{R}_{d}
. Indeed, if it was a unit,
it would give us a relation among the
a_{j,k}
s, which is impossible,
because
R_{d}
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra in the
a_{j,k}
s
and
b_{j,k}
s.

Next, since
R_{d}
is a Dedekind domain, so is its localization

\mathcal{R}_{d}
. Thus, the fact that
\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\notin\mathcal{R}_{d}^{\times}

guarantees that the ideal
\left\langle\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right\rangle

is contained in some non-zero prime ideal
\mathfrak{q}\subseteq\mathcal{R}_{d}
.
As such, considering the
\mathfrak{p}
-adic absolute value
\left|\cdot\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}

on
\mathcal{R}_{d}
, we have that
\left|\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}<1
.

Setting
m\geq n
, our formula from
Proposition

36

the previous proposition gives:

\displaystyle\left\|L_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{n},j}\right)^{m-n}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}r_{\mathbf{n},j}=\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right);

\displaystyle\ll_{n}\left|\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}^{m}

where the constant of proportionality depends on
n
. Thus:

(5.35)

\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}\left\|L_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}\ll_{n}\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}\left|\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}^{m}<1

because
\left|\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}<1
.
This shows that series:

(5.36)

\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}L_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

converges in
\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
the completion of
\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)
with
respect to
\left|\cdot\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}
, albeit pointwise
with respect to
n
. Since the
\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]
s
form a basis for
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\mathfrak{q}}\right)
,
this shows that
1-L_{\mathbf{n}}
is invertible on
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\mathfrak{q}}\right)
.
Moreover, using
Proposition
36

again, we have that:

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}L_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}L_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)+\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}\left(\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right)^{m-n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}+\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{m}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{m}}\right)}{p^{m}}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-m}}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(k\right)\right]

So, let:

(5.37)

S_{n,k}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)/p^{n}}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}+\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{m}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{m}}\right)}{p^{m}}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-m}}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(k\right)\right]

Observe that
S_{n,k}
takes values in
\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)
.
Hence, the linear map
T:\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)

defined by:

(5.38)

T\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}=S_{n,k}

is actually the inverse of
1-L_{\mathbf{n}}
over
\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)
.

Indeed, we don’t even need the absolute value
\left|\cdot\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}

to demonstrate this! Applying
1-L_{\mathbf{n}}
to
S_{n,k}
directly
and using
Proposition
36
,
we get:

\displaystyle\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)\left\{S_{n,k}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)/p^{n}}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}+\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{m}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{m}}\right)}{p^{m}}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-m}}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(k\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)/p^{n}}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}

\displaystyle-\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{m}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{m}}\right)}{p^{m}}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(k\right)\right]_{p}\right)}{p}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-m-1}}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ m+1}\left(k\right)\right]

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{Proposition}\textrm{ }\ref{prop:Kappa shift equation}\right);

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}+\overbrace{\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{m}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{m}}\right)}{p^{m}}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-m}}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(k\right)\right]}^{\textrm{cancel}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{n}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{m}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[k\right]_{p^{m}}\right)}{p^{m}}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-m}}{\equiv}\theta_{p}^{\circ m}\left(k\right)\right]}_{\textrm{cancel}}

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}-\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}+\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]

and we needed no absolute values or topologies to do any of that!
This shows that
S_{n,k}
truly is an element of
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)

that
1-L_{\mathbf{n}}
sends to
\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]
.
Moreover
S_{n,k}
is the
unique
function in
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)

with this property, for if there was another such function, then it
would then be an element of
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\mathfrak{q}}\right)

which
1-L_{\mathbf{n}}
sends to
\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]
,
and thus must be equal to
S_{n,k}
, because of the invertibility
of
1-L_{\mathbf{n}}
over
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\mathfrak{q}}\right)
.

Now, if we consider the quotient by an ideal
I\subseteq R_{d}
,
note that
S_{n,k}
is a perfectly well-defined element of
\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)\right)

if and only if
\left\langle 1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right\rangle\nsubseteq I
,
from which the invertibility of
1-L_{\mathbf{n}}
follows (or doesn’t,
if
\left\langle 1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right\rangle\subseteq I
).

Finally, for the matter of extending to the Wiener algebra, it is
known that for any algebraically closed Banach ring
B
, the set:

(5.39)

\mathcal{B}\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\left\{\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right)}}{\equiv}n\right]:n\geq 0\right\}

is a basis for the Banach space
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)
—the

van der Put basis

[
18
,
16
,
12
,
10
]
.
Every
f\in W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)
admits a unique
van
der Put series
representation with respect to this basis:

(5.40)

f\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}c_{n}\left(f\right)\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right)}}{\equiv}n\right]

where the coefficients
c_{n}\left(f\right)
are called the
van
der Put
coefficients of
f
. Such series necessarily converge in

B
uniformly with respect to
\mathfrak{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. We
will prove that the place
v
we selected guarantees that
\left\|S_{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right),n}\right\|_{p,v}\leq 1

holds for all
n\geq 0
. Because of this, we can then define
\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}

for every element of
C\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)
by:

(5.41)

\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{f\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}c_{n}\left(f\right)S_{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right),n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

Claim 4
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}
be as given in the statement of the lemma. Then,

\left\|S_{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right),n}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}\leq 1

occurs for all
n\geq 0
. The equality is strict if
\mathfrak{q}

is archimedean

Proof of Claim: Letting
n\geq 0
and
k\in\left\{0,\ldots,p^{n}-1\right\}
,
taking supremum norms:

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{m}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\max_{0\leq m<n}\left(\max_{0\leq j<p}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},j}}{p}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}\right)^{m}&\textrm{if }v<\infty\\
\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},j}}{p}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}\right)^{m}&\textrm{else}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}1&\textrm{if }\mathfrak{q}<\infty\\
\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}d^{m}&\textrm{else}\end{cases}

where

(5.42)

d\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},j}}{p}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}

is strictly less then
1
when
\mathfrak{q}
is archimedean. Since

0<d<1
, we have:

(5.43)

\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}d^{m}<\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}d^{m}=\frac{1}{1-d}

a bound independent of
n
, and so:

(5.44)

\sup_{n\geq 0}\sup_{0\leq k<p^{n}}\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{m}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}<\infty

Thus:

\displaystyle\left\|S_{n,k}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}\frac{1}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}+\underbrace{\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{m}\left\{\left[\cdot\overset{p^{n}}{\equiv}k\right]\right\}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}}_{O\left(1\right)\textrm{ with respect to }n,k}

\displaystyle\left(\left\langle 1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right\rangle\nsubseteq I\right);

\displaystyle\ll\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}^{n}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(k\right)}{p^{n}}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}

Here, if
\mathfrak{q}
is non-archimedean, our assumption that:

(5.45)

\max_{0\leq j<p}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},j}}{p}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}\leq 1

then shows that:

(5.46)

\left\|S_{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right),n}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}\leq 1,\textrm{ }\forall n\geq 0

If, on the other hand,
\mathfrak{q}
is archimedean, our assumption
that:

(5.47)

\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},j}}{p}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}<1

forces:

(5.48)

\max_{0\leq j<p}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},j}}{p}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}<1

and hence:

(5.49)

\left\|S_{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right),n}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}\ll\prod_{j=0}^{p-1}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},j}}{p}\right|^{\#_{p:j}\left(n\right)}<1

This proves the Claim.
\checkmark

Finally we conclude, working in our two cases separately. Let
B

denote
\overline{\overline{\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\mathfrak{q}}}}
,
where
\mathfrak{q}
is as given in the statement of the lemma.

I. First, if
\mathfrak{q}
is a non-archimedean absolute value on

B
, then for any
f\in W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)
:

(5.50)

\left\|c_{n}\left(f\right)S_{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right),n}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}=\left|c_{n}\left(f\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}\left\|S_{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right),n}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}\leq\left|c_{n}\left(f\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}

Since
f
is continuous,
\left|c_{n}\left(f\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}\rightarrow 0

in
\mathbb{R}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently:

(5.51)

\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{f\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\overset{\textrm{def}}{=}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}c_{n}\left(f\right)S_{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right),n}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

is a sum of functions in the non-archimedean Banach ring
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)

so that the norm of the
n
th term tends to
0
in
\mathbb{R}

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Because this Banach ring is non-archimedean,
this decay condition then guarantees the convergence of
\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{f\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

in
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)
. Moreover, by the ultrametric
inequality of this Banach space’s norm, we have that:

(5.52)

\left\|\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{f\right\}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}\leq\sup_{n\geq 0}\left(\left|c_{n}\left(f\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}\left\|S_{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right),n}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}\right)\leq\sup_{n\geq 0}\left|c_{n}\left(f\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}

Since the
c_{n}
s decay to
0

\mathfrak{q}
-adically, there
are distinct
m,n
so that
\left|c_{m}\left(f\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}\neq\left|c_{n}\left(f\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}
.
Consequently, the ultrametric inequality tells us that:

(5.53)

\left\|f\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}=\left\|\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}c_{n}\left(f\right)\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right)}}{\equiv}n\right]\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}=\sup_{n\geq 0}\left|c_{n}\left(f\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}

and hence:

(5.54)

\left\|\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{f\right\}\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}\leq\sup_{n\geq 0}\left|c_{n}\left(f\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}=\left\|f\right\|_{p,\mathfrak{q}}

which proves that
\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}
is a continuous
linear operator on
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)
, and hence, that

1-L_{\mathbf{n}}
is invertible on
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)
.

II. Suppose
\mathfrak{q}
is archimedean. Then,
f\in W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)

implies that
f
’s integral converges with respect to
\mathfrak{q}
;
this is:

(5.55)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}f\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)d\mathfrak{z}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{c_{n}\left(f\right)}{p^{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right)}}

Moreover, as shown in
[
18
]
, we have:

(5.56)

\hat{f}\left(t\right)=\sum_{n=\frac{1}{p}\left|t\right|_{p}}^{\infty}\frac{c_{n}\left(f\right)}{p^{\lambda_{p}\left(n\right)}}e^{-2\pi int},\textrm{ }\forall t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

Noting that:

(5.57)

e^{2\pi i\left\{t\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}=\sum_{m=0}^{\max\left\{0,\left|t\right|_{p}-1\right\}}e^{2\pi imt}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{\left|t\right|_{p}}{\equiv}m\right]

we get:

\displaystyle\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{e^{2\pi i\left\{t\cdot\right\}_{p}}\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\left|t\right|_{p}-1}e^{2\pi imt}\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[m\right]_{p}\right)}{p}\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{\left|t\right|_{p}/p}{\equiv}\theta_{p}\left(m\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{m=0}^{\left|t\right|_{p}-1}e^{2\pi imt}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[m\right]_{p}\right)\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{\left|t\right|_{p}/p}{\equiv}\theta_{p}\left(m\right)\right]

Now:

\displaystyle\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{f-\hat{f}\left(0\right)\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left(\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\backslash\left\{0\right\}}\hat{f}\left(t\right)e^{2\pi i\left\{t\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\backslash\left\{0\right\}}\hat{f}\left(t\right)\sum_{m=0}^{\left|t\right|_{p}-1}e^{2\pi imt}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[m\right]_{p}\right)\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{\left|t\right|_{p}/p}{\equiv}\theta_{p}\left(m\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{p^{n}-1}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(\left[m\right]_{p}\right)\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-1}}{\equiv}\theta_{p}\left(m\right)\right]\sum_{\left|t\right|_{p}=p^{n}}\hat{f}\left(t\right)e^{2\pi imt}

Using the summation identity:

(5.58)

\sum_{m=0}^{p^{n}-1}g\left(m\right)=\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\sum_{m=0}^{p^{n-1}-1}g\left(pm+j\right)

we get:

\displaystyle\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{f-\hat{f}\left(0\right)\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\sum_{m=0}^{p^{n-1}-1}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(j\right)\left[\mathfrak{z}\overset{p^{n-1}}{\equiv}m\right]\sum_{\left|t\right|_{p}=p^{n}}\hat{f}\left(t\right)e^{2\pi i\left(pm+j\right)t}

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(j\right)\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\left|t\right|_{p}=p^{n}}\hat{f}\left(t\right)e^{2\pi i\left(p\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n-1}}+j\right)t}

Applying the archimedean absolute value
\left|\cdot\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
we get:

(5.59)

\left|\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{f-\hat{f}\left(0\right)\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}\ll_{\mathbf{n}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\left|t\right|_{p}=p^{n}}\left|\hat{f}\left(t\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}\leq\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\left|\hat{f}\left(t\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}=\left\|f\right\|_{W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)}<\infty

where the equality at the right occurs because of the definition of

W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)
when
\mathfrak{q}
is archimedean.
This shows that
\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{f\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)-\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{\hat{f}\left(0\right)\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)

converges with respect to
\left|\cdot\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}
uniformly
in
\mathfrak{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Since:

(5.60)

\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{\hat{f}\left(0\right)\right\}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\hat{f}\left(0\right)S_{0,0}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\frac{\hat{f}\left(0\right)}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}

which is a constant, we see that
\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{f\right\}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rightarrow B

is continuous.

Now, let
g
denote
\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{f\right\}
.
Then:

(5.61)

g\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=\frac{\hat{f}\left(0\right)}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}+\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(j\right)\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\left|s\right|_{p}=p^{n}}\hat{f}\left(s\right)e^{2\pi i\left(p\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n-1}}+j\right)s}

where, as we just showed, the series converges uniformly in
\mathfrak{z}
.
As such, we can integrate it term-by-term. The main integral is:

(5.62)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi ip\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n-1}}s}e^{-2\pi i\left\{t\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}d\mathfrak{z}

which we deal with by noting that since
s
has
\left|s\right|_{p}=p^{n}
,

ps
has
\left|ps\right|_{p}=p^{n-1}
, and so:

(5.63)

p\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n-1}}s\overset{1}{\equiv}\left\{ps\right\}_{p},\textrm{ }\forall s:\left|s\right|_{p}=p^{n}

Hence:

(5.64)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi ip\left[\mathfrak{z}\right]_{p^{n-1}}s}e^{-2\pi i\left\{t\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}d\mathfrak{z}=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}e^{2\pi i\left\{\left(ps-t\right)\mathfrak{z}\right\}_{p}}d\mathfrak{z}=\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(ps-t\right)=\left[t\overset{1}{\equiv}ps\right]

which will be
1
whenever
ps\overset{1}{\equiv}t
and will be

0
otherwise. We then end up with:

(5.65)

\hat{g}\left(t\right)=\frac{\hat{f}\left(0\right)\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(t\right)}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}+\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(j\right)\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\left|s\right|_{p}=p^{n}}\hat{f}\left(s\right)e^{2\pi ijs}\left[t\overset{1}{\equiv}ps\right]

Applying
Proposition
8
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{\left|s\right|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\hat{f}\left(s\right)e^{2\pi ijs}\left[t\overset{1}{\equiv}ps\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\left|s\right|_{p}\leq p^{n-1}}\sum_{\left|r\right|_{p}\leq p}\hat{f}\left(\frac{s}{p}+r\right)e^{2\pi ij\left(\frac{s}{p}+r\right)}\left[t\overset{1}{\equiv}s\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(p^{n-1}t\right)\sum_{\left|r\right|_{p}\leq p}\hat{f}\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)e^{2\pi ij\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)}

Hence:

\displaystyle\sum_{\left|s\right|_{p}=p^{n}}\hat{f}\left(s\right)e^{2\pi ijs}\left[t\overset{1}{\equiv}ps\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\left|s\right|_{p}\leq p^{n}}\hat{f}\left(s\right)e^{2\pi ijs}\left[t\overset{1}{\equiv}ps\right]-\sum_{\left|s\right|_{p}\leq p^{n-1}}\hat{f}\left(s\right)e^{2\pi ijs}\left[t\overset{1}{\equiv}ps\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(p^{n-1}t\right)-\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(p^{n-2}t\right)\right)\sum_{\left|r\right|_{p}\leq p}\hat{f}\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)e^{2\pi ij\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)}

for all
n\geq 2
, and we get:

\displaystyle\hat{g}\left(t\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\hat{f}\left(0\right)\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(t\right)}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}+\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(j\right)\sum_{\left|s\right|_{p}=p}\hat{f}\left(s\right)e^{2\pi ijs}\underbrace{\left[t\overset{1}{\equiv}ps\right]}_{\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(t\right)}

\displaystyle+\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(j\right)\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\sum_{\left|s\right|_{p}=p^{n}}\hat{f}\left(s\right)e^{2\pi ijs}\left[t\overset{1}{\equiv}ps\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(t\right)\underbrace{\left(\frac{\hat{f}\left(0\right)}{1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)}+\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(j\right)\sum_{\left|s\right|_{p}=p}\hat{f}\left(s\right)e^{2\pi ijs}\right)}_{=C,\textrm{ a constant independent of }t}

\displaystyle+\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(j\right)\underbrace{\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(p^{n+1}t\right)-\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(p^{n}t\right)\right)}_{\textrm{telescoping}}\sum_{\left|r\right|_{p}\leq p}\hat{f}\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)e^{2\pi ij\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)}

\displaystyle=C\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(t\right)+\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(j\right)\left(\underbrace{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(p^{n}t\right)}_{1,\textrm{ }\forall t}-\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(t\right)\right)\sum_{\left|r\right|_{p}\leq p}\hat{f}\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)e^{2\pi ij\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)}

\displaystyle=C\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(t\right)+\left(1-\mathbf{1}_{0}\left(t\right)\right)\frac{r_{\mathbf{n},0}}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\kappa_{\mathbf{n}}\left(j\right)\sum_{\left|r\right|_{p}\leq p}\hat{f}\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)e^{2\pi ij\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)}

and so:

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\left|\hat{g}\left(t\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\left|\sum_{\left|r\right|_{p}\leq p}\hat{f}\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)e^{2\pi ij\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\left|r\right|_{p}\leq p}\sum_{t\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\left|\hat{f}\left(\frac{t}{p}+r\right)\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{\left|r\right|_{p}\leq p}\left\|f\right\|_{W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)}

\displaystyle=p\left\|f\right\|_{W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)}

and so
\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}\left\{f\right\}=g\in W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)
.
Moreover, the above estimate then shows that
\left(1-L_{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{-1}

is continuous with respect to the norm of
W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},B\right)
,
as desired.

Q.E.D.

We now have:

Theorem 11
.

Let
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}
,
and let
I
be a unique solution ideal in
R_{d}
. Then, the
\mathbf{n}
th
formal solution
dY_{\mathbf{n}}\in\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)^{\prime}

relative to the initial condition
\left\{\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}\right\}_{\mathbf{m}\in\left[0,\mathbf{n}\right)}

is uniquely determined by the initial condition whenever
\left\langle 1-\alpha_{\mathbf{n}}\left(0\right)\right\rangle\nsubseteq I
.

Moreover, letting
\left|\cdot\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}
be an absolute
value on
\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}
, if
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{m}}\in W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\mathfrak{q}}}}\right)^{\prime}

for all
\mathbf{m}\in\left[0,\mathbf{n}\right)
, then
dY_{\mathbf{n}}\in W\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\overline{\overline{\textrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{R}_{d}/I\mathcal{R}_{d}\right)_{\mathfrak{q}}}}\right)^{\prime}

whenever:

I. If
\mathfrak{q}
is non-archimedean:

(5.66)

\max_{0\leq k<p}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}}{p}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}\leq 1

II. If
\mathfrak{q}
is archimedean:

(5.67)

\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\left|\frac{r_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},k}}{p}\right|_{\mathfrak{q}}<1

Proof: The first part of the statement follows from
Lemma

4
and
Proposition
35
.
As for being a measure on the wiener algebra, this follows from the
hypothesis on the initial condition and the invertibility of
1-L_{\mathbf{n}}

over the Wiener algebra when (I) and (II) are satisfied, as guaranteed
by
Lemma
4
.
Q.E.D.

Finally, we will show that the Fourier transform of
\hat{Y}_{\mathbf{n}}

we have obtained is in fact a Fourier transform of
X_{\mathbf{n}}
.
We do this with the help of the
p
-adic Dirichlet Kernel.

Definition 40
.

Let
R
be a commutative unital ring whose characteristic, if positive,
is co-prime to
p
. Then, the
p
